The Neverending Story

by Michael Ende.

Appendix A
Variation of the action of the 4D massless superparticle and its equations of
motion

In this appendix we obtain the equations of motion for the massless superparticle in
\text{D}=4

{\cal N}=1
superspace by varying the action of its spinor moving frame formulation

S_{0}^{4\text{D}}=\int_{\mathcal{W}^{1}}\mathcal{L}_{0}^{4\text{D}}=\int_{{\cal W}^{1}}\rho^{\#}\text{E}^{=}=\int_{{\cal W}^{1}}\rho^{\#}\Pi^{\alpha\dot{\beta}}v_{\alpha}^{-}\bar{v}_{\dot{\beta}}^{-}\;,

where
\Pi^{\alpha\dot{\beta}}
is given in (
3.2
).

The exterior derivative of the Lagrangian 1-form

\mathcal{L}_{0}^{4\text{D}}=\rho^{\#}\text{E}^{=}=\rho^{\#}\Pi^{\alpha\dot{\beta}}v_{\alpha}^{-}\bar{v}_{\dot{\beta}}^{-}\penalty\

(A.1)

reads

{\rm d}\mathcal{L}_{0}^{4\text{D}}=-{\rm D}\rho^{\#}\wedge\text{E}^{=}-4i\rho^{\#}\text{E}^{-}\wedge\bar{\text{E}}{}^{-}+\rho^{\#}\text{E}^{+-}\wedge\Omega^{--}+\rho^{\#}\text{E}^{-+}\wedge\bar{\Omega}^{--}\;,

(A.2)

where
\text{E}^{=}
,
\text{E}^{+-}
,
\text{E}^{-+}
,
\text{E}^{-}
and
\bar{\text{E}}^{-}
are defined in (
3.6
)-(
3.9
) and
\Omega^{--}
,
\bar{\Omega}^{--}
are Cartan forms (
3.12
).
Using (
1.46
), (
1.47
) and (
1.50
), we find that

\begin{array}[]{l}\begin{split}\delta\mathcal{L}_{0}^{4\text{D}}&=\iota_{\delta}{\rm D}\rho^{\#}\text{E}^{=}-{\rm D}\rho^{\#}\iota_{\delta}\text{E}^{=}-4i\rho^{\#}\text{E}^{-}\iota_{\delta}\bar{\text{E}}{}^{-}+4i\rho^{\#}\iota_{\delta}\text{E}^{-}\bar{\text{E}}{}^{-}+\\
&+\rho^{\#}\text{E}^{+-}\iota_{\delta}\Omega^{--}-\rho^{\#}\iota_{\delta}\text{E}^{+-}\Omega^{--}+\rho^{\#}\text{E}^{-+}\iota_{\delta}\bar{\Omega}^{--}-\rho^{\#}\iota_{\delta}\text{E}^{-+}\bar{\Omega}^{--}\penalty\ ,\end{split}\end{array}

(A.3)

where we have used

{\rm d}\Pi^{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}=-4i{\rm d}\theta^{\alpha}\wedge d\bar{\theta}{}^{\dot{\alpha}}\qquad\Longrightarrow\qquad\delta\Pi^{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}=+i\delta\theta^{\alpha}{\rm d}\bar{\theta}{}^{\dot{\alpha}}-4i{\rm d}\theta^{\alpha}\delta\bar{\theta}{}^{\dot{\alpha}}+{\rm d}(i_{\delta}\Pi^{\alpha\dot{\alpha}})\;,\qquad

(A.4)

as well as

\begin{array}[]{l}{\rm D}\text{E}^{\#}=-4i\text{E}^{+}\wedge\bar{\text{E}}{}^{+}-\text{E}^{-+}\wedge\Omega^{++}-\text{E}^{+-}\wedge\bar{\Omega}^{++}\qquad\Longrightarrow\\
\qquad\penalty\ \Longrightarrow\quad\delta\text{E}^{\#}=-4i\text{E}^{+}\iota_{\delta}\bar{\text{E}}{}^{+}+4i\iota_{\delta}\text{E}^{+}\bar{\text{E}}{}^{+}+\left(\iota_{\delta}\text{E}^{-+}\Omega^{++}-\text{E}^{-+}\iota_{\delta}\Omega^{++}+\rm c.c.\right)\;,\end{array}

(A.5)

\begin{array}[]{l}\penalty\ \penalty\ {\rm D}\text{E}^{+}=-\text{E}^{-}\wedge\Omega^{++}\qquad\Longrightarrow\qquad\delta\text{E}^{+}=\iota_{\delta}\text{E}^{-}\Omega^{++}-\text{E}^{-}\iota_{\delta}\Omega^{++}+{\rm D}\iota_{\delta}\text{E}^{+}\;,\end{array}

(A.6)

\begin{array}[]{l}\penalty\ \penalty\ {\rm D}\bar{\text{E}}{}^{+}=-\bar{\text{E}}{}^{-}\wedge\bar{\Omega}{}^{++}\qquad\Longrightarrow\qquad\delta\bar{\text{E}}{}^{+}=\iota_{\delta}\bar{\text{E}}^{-}\bar{\Omega}{}^{++}-\bar{\text{E}}{}^{-}\iota_{\delta}\bar{\Omega}{}^{++}+{\rm D}\iota_{\delta}\bar{\text{E}}{}^{+}\;,\end{array}

(A.7)

and

\begin{array}[]{l}{\rm D}\text{E}^{=}=-4i\text{E}^{-}\wedge\bar{\text{E}}{}^{-}+\text{E}^{+-}\wedge\Omega^{--}+\text{E}^{-+}\wedge\bar{\Omega}^{--}\qquad\Longrightarrow\\
\qquad\penalty\ \Longrightarrow\delta\text{E}^{=}=-4i\text{E}^{-}\iota_{\delta}\bar{\text{E}}{}^{-}+4i\iota_{\delta}\text{E}^{-}\bar{\text{E}}{}^{-}+\left(-\iota_{\delta}\text{E}^{+-}\Omega^{--}+\text{E}^{+-}\iota_{\delta}\Omega^{--}+\rm c.c.\right),\end{array}

(A.8)

\begin{array}[]{l}\penalty\ \penalty\ {\rm D}\text{E}^{-}=\text{E}^{+}\wedge\Omega^{--}\qquad\Longrightarrow\qquad\delta\text{E}^{-}=-\iota_{\delta}\text{E}^{+}\Omega^{--}+\text{E}^{+}\iota_{\delta}\Omega^{--}+{\rm D}\iota_{\delta}\text{E}^{-}\;,\end{array}

(A.9)

\begin{array}[]{l}\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ {\rm D}\bar{\text{E}}{}^{-}=\bar{\text{E}}{}^{+}\wedge\bar{\Omega}{}^{--}\qquad\Longrightarrow\qquad\delta\bar{\text{E}}{}^{-}=-\iota_{\delta}\bar{\text{E}}^{+}\bar{\Omega}{}^{--}+\bar{\text{E}}{}^{+}\iota_{\delta}\bar{\Omega}{}^{--}+{\rm D}\iota_{\delta}\bar{\text{E}}{}^{-}\;.\end{array}

(A.10)

These expressions allow to easily find the following set of nontrivial equations of motion of the superparticle in the spinor moving frame formulation:

\displaystyle\dfrac{\delta\mathcal{L}_{0}^{4\text{D}}}{\iota_{\delta}{\rm D}\rho^{\#}}=\dfrac{\delta\mathcal{L}_{0}^{4\text{D}}}{\delta\rho^{\#}}=0\qquad

\displaystyle\Longrightarrow\qquad\text{E}^{=}=0\;,\qquad

(A.11)

\displaystyle\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \dfrac{\delta\mathcal{L}_{0}^{4\text{D}}}{\iota_{\delta}\Omega^{--}}=v_{\alpha}^{+}\dfrac{\delta\mathcal{L}_{0}^{4\text{D}}}{\delta v_{\alpha}^{-}}=0\qquad

\displaystyle\Longrightarrow\qquad\rho^{\#}\text{E}^{+-}=0\;,\qquad

(A.12)

\displaystyle\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \dfrac{\delta\mathcal{L}_{0}^{4\text{D}}}{\iota_{\delta}\bar{\Omega}{}^{--}}=\bar{v}_{\dot{\alpha}}^{+}\dfrac{\delta\mathcal{L}_{0}^{4\text{D}}}{\delta\bar{v}_{\dot{\alpha}}^{-}}=0\qquad

\displaystyle\Longrightarrow\qquad\rho^{\#}\text{E}^{-+}=0\;,\qquad

(A.13)

\displaystyle\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \dfrac{\delta\mathcal{L}_{0}^{4\text{D}}}{\iota_{\delta}\text{E}^{=}}=\dfrac{1}{2}u_{\mu}^{\#}\dfrac{\delta\mathcal{L}_{0}^{4\text{D}}}{\delta x^{\mu}}=0\qquad

\displaystyle\Longrightarrow\qquad{\rm D}\rho^{\#}=0\;,\qquad

(A.14)

\displaystyle\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \dfrac{\delta\mathcal{L}_{0}^{4\text{D}}}{\iota_{\delta}\text{E}^{+-}}=\dfrac{1}{2}u_{\mu}^{-+}\dfrac{\delta\mathcal{L}_{0}^{4\text{D}}}{\delta x^{\mu}}=0\qquad

\displaystyle\Longrightarrow\qquad\rho^{\#}\Omega^{--}=0\;,\qquad

(A.15)

\displaystyle\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \dfrac{\delta\mathcal{L}_{0}^{4\text{D}}}{\iota_{\delta}\text{E}^{-+}}=\dfrac{1}{2}u_{\mu}^{+-}\dfrac{\delta\mathcal{L}_{0}^{4\text{D}}}{\delta x^{\mu}}=0\qquad

\displaystyle\Longrightarrow\qquad\rho^{\#}\bar{\Omega}^{--}=0\;,\qquad

(A.16)

\displaystyle\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \dfrac{\delta\mathcal{L}_{0}^{4\text{D}}}{\iota_{\delta}\text{E}^{-}}=-v^{\alpha+}\dfrac{\delta\mathcal{L}_{0}^{4\text{D}}}{\delta\theta^{\alpha}}=0\qquad

\displaystyle\Longrightarrow\qquad\rho^{\#}\bar{\text{E}}^{-}=0\;,\qquad

(A.17)

\displaystyle\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \dfrac{\delta\mathcal{L}_{0}^{4\text{D}}}{\iota_{\delta}\bar{\text{E}}^{-}}=-\bar{v}{}^{\dot{\alpha}+}\dfrac{\delta\mathcal{L}_{0}^{4\text{D}}}{\delta\bar{\theta}{}^{\dot{\alpha}}}=0\qquad

\displaystyle\Longrightarrow\qquad\rho^{\#}\text{E}^{-}=0\;.\qquad

(A.18)

Appendix B
3D massless superparticle from dimensional reduction of 4D massless superparticle

In this appendix we discuss the derivation of the
\text{D}=3
massless superparticle action from the action of
\text{D}=4
massless superparticle in its spinor frame formulation. This serves as a useful and instructive warm up exercise, illustrating the general approach to dimensional reduction that have been applied to more complex systems in section
4
.

B.1

Brink-Schwarz action

To perform the dimensional reduction of the Brink-Schwarz action for massless superparticle, Eq. (
4.9
), we can just isolate one of the momentum components (say,
p_{2}
, a choice is made here for convenience in later discussions),

p_{\mu}=(p_{\tilde{\mu}},p_{2})\;,\qquad{\tilde{\mu}}=0,1,3\qquad\longleftrightarrow\qquad\mu=0,1,2,3\;,

(B.1)

and setting this component to zero in (
4.9
),

p_{2}=0\;.

(B.2)

In such a way we obtain the
\text{D}=3
massless superparticle action

S_{\rm BS}^{3\rm D}=\int_{\mathcal{W}^{1}}\left(p_{\tilde{\mu}}\Pi^{\tilde{\mu}}+\frac{e}{2}p_{\tilde{\mu}}p^{\tilde{\mu}}\right)\;.

(B.3)

B.2

Spinor moving frame formulation

As in the spinor moving frame formulation the role of additional momentum variable are taken by bilinear of bosonic spinors,
v_{\alpha}^{-}
and
\bar{v}_{\dot{\alpha}}^{-}
, it is natural to expect that similar dimensional reduction can be performed just by choosing a suitable ansatz for the
\text{D}=4
harmonics in terms of
\text{D}=3
ones. Actually the ansatz consists in imposing reality conditions

v_{\alpha}^{-}=\bar{v}_{\dot{\alpha}}^{-}={\rm v}_{\alpha}^{-}\;,\qquad v_{\alpha}^{+}=\bar{v}_{\dot{\alpha}}^{+}={\rm v}_{\alpha}^{+}\;,

(B.4)

were we have introduced real 3D spinor moving frame variables
{\rm v}_{\alpha}^{\pm}
which form the SL
(2,{\mathbb{R}})
valued matrix, i.e. obey

{\rm v}^{-\alpha}{\rm v}_{\alpha}^{+}=1\;,\qquad({\rm v}_{\alpha}^{\pm})^{*}={\rm v}_{\alpha}^{\pm}\;.

(B.5)

This choice is invariant under SL
(2,{\mathbb{R}})=\text{Spin}(1,2)
, subgroup of SL
(2,{\mathbb{C}})=\text{Spin}(1,3)
.

Using the split of the
\text{D}=4
relativistic Pauli matrices (
4.5
) as in
(
4.6
) and identifying the
\text{D}=3
gamma matrices as in (
4.7
), (
4.8
), we find that the ansatz (
B.4
) and (
2.20
), (
2.21
) implies

u_{2}^{=}=0\;,\qquad u_{2}^{\#}=0\;,\qquad u_{2}^{-+}=-u_{2}^{+-}=(u_{2}^{+-})^{*}\;,

(B.6)

while the real 3-vectors, obtained from
0,1,3
components of the 4D moving frame vectors, are identified with the components of the 3D moving frame vectors

{\rm u}_{\tilde{\mu}}^{=}={\rm v}^{-}\gamma_{\tilde{\mu}}{\rm v}^{-}={\rm v}^{-\alpha}\gamma_{\tilde{\mu}\,\alpha\beta}{\rm v}^{-\beta}\,,\qquad{\rm u}_{\tilde{\mu}}^{\#}={\rm v}^{+}\gamma_{\tilde{\mu}}{\rm v}^{+}\,,\qquad u_{\tilde{\mu}}^{\perp}={\rm v}^{+}\gamma_{\tilde{\mu}}{\rm v}^{-}\,\qquad

(B.7)

as follows

u_{\tilde{\mu}}^{=}={\rm u}_{\tilde{\mu}}^{=}\,,\qquad u_{\tilde{\mu}}^{\#}={\rm u}_{\tilde{\mu}}^{\#}\,,\qquad u_{\tilde{\mu}}^{-+}=u_{\tilde{\mu}}^{+-}=u_{\tilde{\mu}}^{\perp}\,.\qquad

(B.8)

Now, let us turn to the 4D massless superparticle action (
3.5
). With the ansatz
(
B.4
), which results in (
B.6
), the component
\mu=2
of the
\Pi^{\mu}
1-forms,

\Pi^{2}=\text{d}x^{2}-i{\rm d}\theta\sigma^{2}\bar{\theta}+i\theta\sigma^{2}{\rm d}\bar{\theta}={\rm d}x^{2}-\epsilon_{\alpha\beta}({\rm d}\theta^{\alpha}\bar{\theta}^{\beta}-\theta^{\alpha}{\rm d}\bar{\theta}^{\beta})\;,

(B.9)

just disappears from the action. Then, the resulted expression can be identified as the 3D massless superparticle action in its spinor moving frame formulation

S_{0}^{3\rm D}=\int_{\mathcal{W}^{1}}\rho^{\#}{\rm v}_{\alpha}^{-}\bar{{\rm v}}_{\beta}^{-}\Pi^{\alpha\beta}=\int_{\mathcal{W}^{1}}\rho^{\#}{\rm u}^{=}_{\tilde{\mu}}\Pi^{\tilde{\mu}}=\int_{\mathcal{W}^{1}}\rho^{\#}{\rm E}^{=}\;.

(B.10)

Here we have introduced 3D VA 1-forms

\qquad\Pi^{\tilde{\mu}}={\rm d}x^{\tilde{\mu}}-i{\rm d}\theta{\gamma}^{\tilde{\mu}}\bar{\theta}+i\theta{\gamma}^{\tilde{\mu}}{\rm d}\bar{\theta}\;,

(B.11)

as well as one of 3D counterparts of the pull-backs of the 4D supervielbein forms (
3.6
)-(
3.8
) adapted to the embedding,

{\rm E}^{=}=\Pi^{\tilde{\mu}}{\rm u}_{\tilde{\mu}}^{=}=\frac{1}{2}{\rm u}_{\alpha\beta}^{=}\Pi^{\alpha\beta}=\Pi^{\alpha\beta}{\rm v}_{\alpha}^{-}{\rm v}_{\beta}^{-}\penalty\ ,

(B.12)

{\rm E}^{\#}=\Pi^{\tilde{\mu}}{\rm u}_{\tilde{\mu}}^{\#}=\frac{1}{2}{\rm u}_{\alpha\beta}^{\#}\Pi^{\alpha\beta}=\Pi^{\alpha\beta}{\rm v}_{\alpha}^{+}{\rm v}_{\beta}^{+}\penalty\ ,

(B.13)

\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ {\rm E}^{\perp}=\Pi^{\tilde{\mu}}{\rm u}_{\tilde{\mu}}^{\perp}=\frac{1}{2}{\rm u}_{\alpha\beta}^{\perp}\Pi^{\alpha\beta}=\Pi^{\alpha\beta}{\rm v}_{(\alpha}^{-}{\rm v}_{\beta)}^{+}\penalty\ .

(B.14)

B.3

Some properties and applications of SL(2,
{\mathbb{R}}
)/SO(2) Cartan forms

Inverting the relations (
4.50
) and (
4.51
)with using the unity decomposition

\delta_{\mu}^{\penalty\ \penalty\ \nu}=u_{\mu}^{0}u^{0\nu}-u_{\mu}^{1}u^{1\nu}-u_{\mu}^{2}u^{2\nu}\penalty\ ,

(B.15)

we find

\left.\begin{matrix}{\rm d}{\rm u}^{0}_{\alpha\beta}={\rm u}^{1}_{\alpha\beta}f^{1}+{\rm u}^{2}_{\alpha\beta}f^{2}\;,\cr{\rm d}{\rm u}^{1}_{\alpha\beta}={\rm u}^{0}_{\alpha\beta}f^{1}+{\rm u}^{2}_{\alpha\beta}f^{qq},\cr{\rm d}{\rm u}^{2}_{\alpha\beta}={\rm u}^{0}_{\alpha\beta}f^{2}-{\rm u}^{1}_{\alpha\beta}f^{qq}.\cr\end{matrix}\right\}\qquad\Longleftrightarrow\qquad\begin{cases}\text{d}{\rm u}^{0}_{\alpha\beta}={\rm u}^{1}_{\alpha\beta}f^{1}+{\rm u}^{2}_{\alpha\beta}f^{2}\;,\cr{\rm D}{\rm u}^{1}_{\alpha\beta}={\rm d}{\rm u}^{1}_{\alpha\beta}-{\rm u}^{2}_{\alpha\beta}f^{qq}={\rm u}^{0}_{\alpha\beta}f^{1},\cr{\rm D}{\rm u}^{2}_{\alpha\beta}={\rm d}{\rm u}^{2}_{\alpha\beta}+{\rm u}^{1}_{\alpha\beta}f^{qq}={\rm u}^{0}_{\alpha\beta}f^{2}.\cr\end{cases}

(B.16)

and

f^{11}=\frac{1}{2}f^{qq}-\frac{1}{2}f^{1}\;,\qquad f^{22}=\frac{1}{2}f^{qq}+\frac{1}{2}f^{1}\;,\qquad f^{12}=\frac{1}{2}f^{2}\;.\qquad

(B.17)

Notice also that

{\rm d}f^{qp}=-\epsilon_{q^{\prime}p^{\prime}}f^{qq^{\prime}}\wedge f^{pp^{\prime}}=f^{q}{}_{p^{\prime}}\wedge f^{pp^{\prime}}\;.

(B.18)

In particular this implies

{\rm d}f^{qq}=-\epsilon_{q^{\prime}p^{\prime}}f^{qq^{\prime}}\wedge f^{qp^{\prime}}=2(f^{11}-f^{22})\wedge f^{12}\;,

(B.19)

{\rm d}(f^{11}-f^{22})=2f^{qq}\wedge f^{12}\;,

(B.20)

{\rm d}f^{12}=f^{11}\wedge f^{22}=-\frac{1}{2}f^{qq}\wedge(f^{11}-f^{22})\;.

(B.21)

Equivalently, we can write this relations as

{\rm D}f^{1}={\rm d}f^{1}+f^{qq}\wedge f^{2}=0\;,\qquad{\rm D}f^{2}={\rm d}f^{2}-f^{qq}\wedge f^{1}=0\;,\qquad{\rm d}f^{qq}=-f^{1}\wedge f^{2}\;.\qquad

(B.22)

In terms of covariant derivatives and SO
(2)
Cartan forms the derivatives of 3D spinor harmonics are

{\rm D}{\rm v}_{\alpha}^{1}={\rm d}{\rm v}_{\alpha}^{1}+\frac{1}{2}{\rm v}_{\alpha}^{2}f^{qq}=\frac{1}{2}{\rm v}_{\alpha}^{1}f^{2}+\frac{1}{2}{\rm v}_{\alpha}^{2}f^{1}\;,\qquad{\rm D}{\rm v}_{\alpha}^{2}={\rm d}{\rm v}_{\alpha}^{2}-\frac{1}{2}{\rm v}_{\alpha}^{1}f^{qq}=\frac{1}{2}{\rm v}_{\alpha}^{1}f^{1}-\frac{1}{2}{\rm v}_{\alpha}^{2}f^{2}\;.\qquad

(B.23)

Using the above equations we can easily find

{\rm D}{\cal E}^{1}=\frac{1}{2}{\cal E}^{1}\wedge f^{2}+\frac{1}{2}{\cal E}^{2}\wedge f^{1}\;,\qquad{\rm D}{\cal E}^{2}=\frac{1}{2}{\cal E}^{1}\wedge f^{1}-\frac{1}{2}{\cal E}^{2}\wedge f^{2}\;,\qquad

(B.24)

which imply

\qquad{\rm D}({\cal E}^{1}-i{\cal E}^{2})=\frac{1}{2}({\cal E}^{1}+i{\cal E}^{2})\wedge(f^{2}-if^{1})\;,

(B.25)

\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ {\rm D}(\bar{{\cal E}}^{1}+i\bar{{\cal E}}^{2})=\frac{1}{2}(\bar{{\cal E}}^{1}-i\bar{{\cal E}}^{2})\wedge(f^{2}+if^{1})\;.

(B.26)

These equations and the Ricci identities for covariant derivatives of matrix fields (
4.64
), (
4.65
)

{\rm D}{\rm D}{\mathbb{Z}}=if^{1}\wedge f^{2}{\mathbb{Z}}+[{\mathbb{F}},{\mathbb{Z}}]\;,

(B.27)

\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ {\rm D}{\rm D}\bar{{\mathbb{Z}}}=-if^{1}\wedge f^{2}\bar{{\mathbb{Z}}}+[{\mathbb{F}},\bar{{\mathbb{Z}}}]\;,

(B.28)

\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ {\rm D}{\rm D}{\Psi}=-\frac{i}{2}f^{1}\wedge f^{2}{\Psi}+[{\mathbb{F}},{\Psi}]\;,

(B.29)

\penalty\ \penalty\ {\rm D}{\rm D}\bar{{\Psi}}=\frac{i}{2}f^{1}\wedge f^{2}\bar{{\Psi}}+[{\mathbb{F}},\bar{{\Psi}}]\;,

(B.30)

are useful to prove
\kappa
-symmetry of the 3D mD
0
action. The same applies to

{\rm d}{\rm E}^{0}={\rm E}^{1}\wedge f^{1}+{\rm E}^{2}\wedge f^{2}-2i({\cal E}^{1}\wedge\bar{{\cal E}}^{1}+{\cal E}^{2}\wedge\bar{{\cal E}}^{2})\;,

(B.31)

and

{\rm d}\left({\rm d}\theta^{\alpha}\bar{\theta}_{\alpha}-\theta^{\alpha}{\rm d}\bar{\theta}_{\alpha}\right)=-2{\cal E}^{1}\wedge\bar{{\cal E}}^{2}+2{\cal E}^{2}\wedge\bar{{\cal E}}^{1}\;.\qquad

(B.32)

The formal exterior derivative of the Lagrangian 1-form of this system reads

\begin{array}[]{l}\begin{split}{\rm d}{\cal L}^{3\rm D}_{\rm D0}&=m{\rm d}\left({\rm E}^{0}+{\rm d}\theta^{\alpha}\bar{\theta}_{\alpha}-\theta^{\alpha}{\rm d}\bar{\theta}_{\alpha}\right)=\\
&=-2im({\cal E}^{1}+i{\cal E}^{2})\wedge(\bar{{\cal E}}^{1}-i\bar{{\cal E}}^{2})+m{\rm E}^{1}\wedge f^{1}+m{\rm E}^{2}\wedge f^{2}=\\
&=-2im({\cal E}^{2}-i{\cal E}^{1})\wedge(\bar{{\cal E}}^{2}+i\bar{{\cal E}}^{1})+m{\rm E}^{1}\wedge f^{1}+m{\rm E}^{2}\wedge f^{2}\;.\end{split}\end{array}

(B.33)

Appendix C
Single D0-brane in spinor moving frame formulation and its
\kappa
-symmetry

The action of the moving frame formulation of the 10D D
0
-brane in flat type IIA superspace, which also appears as a part of the multiple D
0
-brane action (
5.27
) describing the center of mass dynamics of this system, reads
[
168
]

S_{\text{D}0}=\int_{\mathcal{W}^{1}}{\cal L}_{\text{D}0}=m\int_{\mathcal{W}^{1}}\text{E}^{0}-im\int_{\mathcal{W}^{1}}\left(\text{d}\theta^{1\alpha}\theta_{\alpha}^{2}-\theta^{1\alpha}\text{d}\theta_{\alpha}^{2}\right)\penalty\ .

(C.1)

Here
\text{d}=\text{d}\tau\partial/\partial\tau=:\text{d}\tau\partial_{\tau}
,
\tau
is proper time variable parametrizing the D
0
-brane worldline

\mathcal{W}^{1}
defined as a line in target
\text{D}=10
type IIA superspace
\Sigma^{(10|32)}
with
10
bosonic and
16+16=32
fermionic coordinates

z^{M}=(x^{\mu},\theta^{1\alpha},\theta_{\alpha}^{2})\qquad

(C.2)

in terms of coordinate functions

z^{M}(\tau)=(x^{\mu}(\tau),\theta^{1\alpha}(\tau),\theta_{\alpha}^{2}(\tau))\;,

(C.3)

\mathcal{W}^{1}\in\Sigma^{(10|32)}:\qquad z^{M}=z^{M}(\tau)\;.

(C.4)

The constant
m
entering both terms of (
C.1
) is the mass of D
0
-brane and
\text{E}^{0}
is the contraction

\text{E}^{0}=\Pi^{\mu}u^{0}_{\mu}\qquad

(C.5)

of the pull-back to the worldline of the 10D VA
1
-form

\Pi^{\mu}=\text{d}x^{\mu}-i\text{d}\theta^{1}\sigma^{\mu}\theta^{1}-i\text{d}\theta^{2}\tilde{\sigma}^{\mu}\theta^{2}\qquad

(C.6)

with the vector field
u_{\mu}^{0}=u_{\mu}^{0}(\tau)
.
The pull-back of a differential form on target superspace is obtained by substituting the coordinate functions for coordinates; so that
Eq. (
C.5
) actually includes

\Pi^{\mu}=\text{d}\tau\Pi_{\tau}^{\mu}=\text{d}x^{\mu}(\tau)-i\text{d}\theta^{1}(\tau)\sigma^{\mu}\theta^{1}(\tau)-i\text{d}\theta^{2}(\tau)\tilde{\sigma}^{\mu}\theta^{2}(\tau)\;.

(C.7)

Notice that, to simplify notation, below and along this thesis, we use the same symbols for the differential forms on the target superspace and their pull-backs to the worldline
\mathcal{W}^{1}
. The same applies to the superspace coordinates (
C.2
) and the coordinate functions (
C.3
). Particularly, in the second term of (
C.1
)
\theta^{1\alpha}
and
\theta_{\alpha}^{2}
denote
\theta^{1\alpha}(\tau)
and
\theta_{\alpha}^{2}(\tau)
.

A very important property of the action (
C.1
) is that, besides manifest
\text{D}=10

\mathcal{N}=2
spacetime supersymmetry, it is also invariant under the following local fermionic
\kappa
-symmetry transformations

\begin{array}[]{ccl}\penalty\ \delta_{\kappa}\theta^{1\alpha}=\kappa^{q}v_{q}^{\alpha}\penalty\ ,\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \delta_{\kappa}\theta_{\alpha}^{2}=-\kappa^{q}v_{\alpha}{}^{q}\penalty\ ,\\
\penalty\ \delta_{\kappa}x^{\mu}=i\delta_{\kappa}\theta^{1}\sigma^{\mu}\theta^{1}+i\delta_{\kappa}\theta^{2}\tilde{\sigma}{}^{\mu}\theta^{2}\penalty\ ,\\
\qquad\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \delta_{\kappa}v_{\alpha}{}^{q}=0\qquad\Longrightarrow\qquad\delta_{\kappa}u^{i}_{\mu}=\delta_{\kappa}u^{0}_{\mu}=0\penalty\ \penalty\ ,\end{array}

(C.8)

where
\kappa^{q}=\kappa^{q}(\tau)
with
q=1,\ldots,16
are arbitrary fermionic functions.

The Lagrangian 1-form of the action (
C.1
) of single D
0
-brane in flat 10D type IIA superspace
[
168
]
:

\mathcal{L}_{\text{D}0}=m\text{E}^{0}-im(\text{d}\theta^{1}\theta^{2}-\theta^{1}\text{d}\theta^{2})\penalty\ .

(C.9)

The formal exterior derivative of
\text{E}^{0}=\Pi^{\mu}u^{0}_{\mu}
in the first term of the Lagrangian form is given by

\text{d}\text{E}^{0}=\text{E}^{i}\wedge\Omega^{i}-i\left(\text{E}^{1q}\wedge\text{E}^{1q}+\text{E}^{2}_{q}\wedge\text{E}^{2}_{q}\right)\penalty\ ,

(C.10)

where

\text{E}^{i}=\Pi^{\mu}u^{i}_{\mu}\;,\qquad\text{E}^{1q}=\text{d}\theta^{1\alpha}\,v_{\alpha}{}^{q}\;,\qquad\text{E}_{q}^{2}=\text{d}\theta_{\alpha}^{2}v_{q}{}^{\alpha}\;.\qquad

(C.11)

To find (
C.10
) we have used

\text{d}\Pi^{\mu}=-i\text{d}\theta^{1}\sigma^{\mu}\wedge\text{d}\theta^{1}-i\text{d}\theta^{2}\tilde{\sigma}^{\mu}\wedge\text{d}\theta^{2}\penalty\

(C.12)

as well as Eqs. (
5.12
) and (
5.18
).

The derivative of the second, Wess-Zumino term of the D
0
-brane action is

-2im\text{d}\theta^{1\alpha}\wedge\text{d}\theta_{\alpha}^{2}=-2im\text{E}^{1q}\wedge\text{E}_{q}^{2}\;,

(C.13)

where we have used
\delta_{\alpha}^{\penalty\ \penalty\ \beta}=v_{\alpha}^{q}v_{q}^{\beta}
. Collecting (
C.10
) and (
C.13
) we find that the formal exterior derivative of the Lagrangian form of single D
0
-brane can be written as

\text{d}{\cal L}_{\text{D}0}=m\text{E}^{i}\wedge\Omega^{i}-im(\text{E}^{1q}+\text{E}^{2}_{q})\wedge(\text{E}^{1q}+\text{E}^{2}_{q})\penalty\ ,

(C.14)

where
\Omega^{i}
is the covariant Cartan form defined in (
5.16
). Then, using the Lie derivative formula (
1.50
), we find

\displaystyle\delta\mathcal{L}_{\text{D}0}=m\left(\text{E}^{i}\iota_{\delta}\Omega^{i}-\iota_{\delta}\text{E}^{i}\Omega^{i}\right)-2im\left(\text{E}^{1q}+\text{E}^{2}_{q}\right)\left(\iota_{\delta}\text{E}^{1q}+\iota_{\delta}\text{E}^{2}_{q}\right)\penalty\ ,

(C.15)

where
\iota_{\delta}\Omega^{i}
defines essential variation of the spinor frame variable by

\delta v_{\alpha}{}^{q}=\iota_{\delta}\text{D}v_{\alpha}{}^{q}=\frac{1}{2}\gamma^{i}_{qp}v_{\alpha}{}^{p}\iota_{\delta}\Omega^{i}\penalty\ .

(C.16)

This equation can be obtained from the
\iota_{\delta}
contraction of (
5.19
) by setting
\iota_{\delta}\Omega^{ij}=0
.

To conclude, let us note that the local fermionic
\kappa
-symmetry transformations (
C.8
) leaving invariant the D
0
-brane action (
C.1
) can be written in the following equivalent form
(
\iota_{\kappa}{\rm d}:=\delta_{\kappa}
)

\begin{array}[]{c}\iota_{\kappa}\Pi^{\mu}=\delta_{\kappa}x^{\mu}-i\delta_{\kappa}\theta^{1}\sigma^{\mu}\theta^{1}-i\delta_{\kappa}\theta^{2}\tilde{\sigma}{}^{\mu}\theta^{2}=0\qquad\Longrightarrow\qquad\iota_{\kappa}\text{E}^{0}=0\penalty\ ,\qquad\iota_{\kappa}\text{E}^{i}=0\penalty\ ,\\
\iota_{\kappa}\Omega^{i}=0\penalty\ ,\qquad\iota_{\kappa}\Omega^{ij}=0\penalty\ ,\\
\iota_{\kappa}\text{E}^{1q}=-\iota_{\kappa}\text{E}^{2}_{q}=\kappa^{q}\qquad\Longrightarrow\qquad\iota_{\kappa}(\text{E}^{1q}+\text{E}^{2}_{q})=0\;.\end{array}

(C.17)

Indeed, it is not difficult to check that substituting the above defined
\iota_{\kappa}
for
\iota_{\delta}
in (
C.15
), we find
\delta_{\kappa}{\cal L}_{\text{D}0}=0
.

Appendix D
Multiple D0-brane action and its worldline supersymmetry

In this appendix we present some details of the proof of the worldline supersymmetry of the candidate mD
0
action (
5.27
).

D.1

Formal exterior derivative of the Lagrangian form of the mD0 action

The first stage is to calculate the formal exterior derivative of the Lagrangian form of the action (
5.27
),

\begin{array}[]{c}\begin{split}\mathcal{L}_{\text{mD}0}&=m\text{E}^{0}-im(\text{d}\theta^{1}\theta^{2}-\theta^{1}\text{d}\theta^{2})\penalty\ +\\
&+\frac{1}{\mu^{6}}\left[\text{tr}\left({\mathbb{P}}^{i}\text{D}{\mathbb{X}}^{i}+4i{\bm{\Psi}}_{q}\text{D}{\bm{\Psi}}_{q}\right)+\frac{2}{\cal M}\text{E}^{0}{\cal H}-\frac{\text{d}{\cal M}}{{\cal M}}\text{tr}({\mathbb{P}}^{i}{\mathbb{X}}^{i})\penalty\ +\right.\\
&\left.+\frac{1}{\sqrt{2{\cal M}}}(\text{E}{}^{q1}-\text{E}{}^{2}_{q})\text{tr}\left(-4i(\gamma^{i}{\bm{\Psi}})_{q}{\mathbb{P}}^{i}+{1\over 2}(\gamma^{ij}{\bm{\Psi}})_{q}[{\mathbb{X}}^{i},{\mathbb{X}}^{j}]\right)\right]\penalty\ .\end{split}\end{array}

(D.1)

Let us recall that in it
{\cal H}
is given in Eq. (
5.28
), the covariant derivatives D of the bosonic and fermionic Hermitian traceless
N\times N
matrix fields are defined in (
5.32
) and (
5.33
)
with the use of
1
d gauge field 1-form
\mathbb{A}=\text{d}\tau\mathbb{A}_{\tau}
and Cartan forms (
5.16
).

To compute the exterior derivative of (
D.1
), we have to use the Ricci identities

\displaystyle\text{DD}{\mathbb{X}}^{i}=\Omega^{i}\wedge\Omega^{j}\,{\mathbb{X}}^{j}+[{\mathbb{F}},{\mathbb{X}}^{i}]\penalty\ ,\qquad\text{DD}{\bm{\Psi}}_{q}=\frac{1}{4}\,\Omega^{i}\wedge\Omega^{j}\,(\gamma^{ij}{\bm{\Psi}})_{q}+[{\mathbb{F}},{\bm{\Psi}}_{q}]\penalty\ ,

(D.2)

where
\mathbb{F}=\text{d}\mathbb{A}-\mathbb{A}\wedge\mathbb{A}
is the formal 2-form field strength of the 1d gauge field
\mathbb{A}
. Eqs. (
D.2
) are obtained using the Maurer-Cartan equations (
5.35
).

After some algebra, the exterior derivative of the multiple D
0
-branes Lagrangian form (
D.1
) can be found to be

\begin{array}[]{c}\begin{split}\mu^{6}\text{d}{\cal L}_{\text{mD}0}&=\mu^{6}m\text{E}^{i}\wedge\Omega^{i}-i\mu^{6}m(\text{E}^{1q}+\text{E}^{2}_{q})\wedge(\text{E}^{1q}+\text{E}^{2}_{q})+\Omega^{i}\wedge\Omega^{j}\,{\rm tr}({\mathbb{P}}^{i}{\mathbb{X}}^{j}+i\bm{\Psi}\gamma^{ij}\bm{\Psi})\penalty\ +\\
&+{\rm tr}\left[{\mathbb{F}}\left(\left[{\mathbb{X}}^{i},{\mathbb{P}}^{i}\right]-4i\{\bm{\Psi}_{q},\bm{\Psi}_{q}\}\right)\right]-{\rm tr}(\text{D}{\mathbb{P}}^{i}\wedge\text{D}{\mathbb{X}}^{i})-4i{\rm tr}(\text{D}{\bm{\Psi}}_{q}\wedge\text{D}{\bm{\Psi}}_{q})\penalty\ +\\
&+\frac{2}{{\cal M}}\text{E}^{i}\wedge\Omega^{i}{\cal H}-i\frac{2}{{\cal M}}(\text{E}^{1q}\wedge\text{E}^{1q}+\text{E}^{2}_{q}\wedge\text{E}^{2}_{q}){\cal H}-\frac{1}{2\sqrt{2{\cal M}}}(\text{E}^{1q}+\text{E}_{q}^{2})\gamma^{i}_{qp}i\nu_{p}\wedge\Omega^{i}\penalty\ +\\
&+\frac{2}{{\cal M}}\left(1-\frac{1}{\mu^{6}}\frac{{\cal M}^{\prime}}{{\cal M}}{\cal H}\right)\text{E}^{0}\wedge\text{d}{\cal H}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{2{\cal M}}}(\text{E}^{1q}-\text{E}_{q}^{2})\wedge i\text{D}\nu_{q}+\frac{1}{\mu^{6}}\frac{{\cal M}^{\prime}}{{\cal M}}\text{d}{\cal K}\wedge\text{d}{\cal H}\penalty\ +\\
&+\frac{1}{\mu^{6}}\,\frac{1}{2\sqrt{2{\cal M}}}\frac{{\cal M}^{\prime}}{{\cal M}}(\text{E}^{1q}-\text{E}_{q}^{2})i\nu_{q}\wedge\text{d}{\cal H}\penalty\ ,\end{split}\end{array}

(D.3)

where
{\cal K}:={\rm tr}({\mathbb{X}}^{i}{\mathbb{P}}^{i})
,
\nu_{q}
and
{\cal H}
are defined in (
5.55
) and (
5.28
), respectively. The derivatives of these “blocks”, which also enter (
D.3
), read

\text{d}{\cal K}={\rm tr}(\text{D}{\mathbb{X}}^{i}\,{\mathbb{P}}^{i}+{\mathbb{X}}^{i}\text{D}{\mathbb{P}}^{i})\;,

(D.4)

i\text{D}\nu_{q}={\rm tr}\left(-4i(\gamma^{i}\bm{\Psi})_{q}\text{D}{\mathbb{P}}^{i}-4i(\gamma^{i}\text{D}\bm{\Psi})_{q}{\mathbb{P}}^{i}-\text{D}{\mathbb{X}}^{i}[(\gamma^{ij}\bm{\Psi})_{q},{\mathbb{X}}^{j}]+{1\over 2}(\gamma^{ij}\text{D}\bm{\Psi})_{q}[{\mathbb{X}}^{i},{\mathbb{X}}^{j}]\right)\penalty\ ,

(D.5)

\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \text{d}{\cal H}=\text{tr}\left({\mathbb{P}}^{i}\text{D}{\mathbb{P}}^{i}+\frac{1}{16}\text{D}{\mathbb{X}}^{i}[[{\mathbb{X}}^{i},{\mathbb{X}}^{j}],{\mathbb{X}}^{j}]-\text{D}{\mathbb{X}}^{i}\gamma^{i}_{pq}\{{\bm{\Psi}}_{p},{\bm{\Psi}}_{q}\}-2\,\text{D}{\bm{\Psi}}_{q}[(\gamma^{i}{\bm{\Psi}})_{q},{\mathbb{X}}^{i}]\,\right)\penalty\ .

(D.6)

D.2

Worldline supersymmetry (
\kappa
-symmetry) transformations of the center of mass variables

The previous experience with lower dimensional counterparts of the mD
0
system (see chapter
4
) suggested to assume that the worldline supersymmetry acts on the center of mass variables of the mD
0
system (i.e. on the superspace coordinate functions and spinor frame variables)
as the
\kappa
-symmetry of the single D
0
-brane action (see appendix
C
) acts on their single brane counterparts. Namely, we set

62
62

62

The re-scaling of fermionic function
{\kappa^{q}}\mapsto\dfrac{\kappa^{q}}{\sqrt{2}}
is performed to simplify the worldline supersymmetry transformation rules of the matrix fields.

\begin{array}[]{ccl}\qquad\qquad\qquad\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \iota_{\kappa}\Pi^{\mu}=0&\Longrightarrow&\qquad\iota_{\kappa}\text{E}^{0}=0\;,\qquad\iota_{\kappa}\text{E}^{i}=0\;,\\
\iota_{\kappa}\Omega^{i}=0\penalty\ ,\qquad\iota_{\kappa}\Omega^{ij}=0&\Longrightarrow&\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \delta_{\kappa}u^{0}_{\mu}=0\;,\qquad\delta_{\kappa}u^{i}_{\mu}=0\;,\qquad\delta_{\kappa}v_{\alpha}{}^{q}=0\;,\end{array}

(D.7)

and

\iota_{\kappa}\text{E}^{1q}=-\iota_{\kappa}\text{E}^{2}_{q}=\frac{\kappa^{q}}{\sqrt{2}}\qquad\Longrightarrow\qquad\delta_{\kappa}\theta^{1\alpha}=\frac{\kappa^{q}}{\sqrt{2}}v_{q}^{\alpha}\penalty\ ,\qquad\delta_{\kappa}\theta_{\alpha}^{2}=-\dfrac{\kappa^{q}}{\sqrt{2}}v_{\alpha}{}^{q}\penalty\ .

(D.8)

These expressions are equivalent to (
5.37
), but they are more convenient to use in our method of calculation of the variation of Lagrangian form.

Then, using the Lie derivative formula with (
D.7
), (
D.8
) and furthermore identifying in it

\iota_{\kappa}\text{D}=\delta_{\kappa}\penalty\ ,\qquad\iota_{\kappa}{\mathbb{F}}=\delta_{\kappa}{\mathbb{A}}\penalty\ ,\qquad\iota_{\kappa}{\mathbb{A}}=0\penalty\ ,

(D.9)

we find that, modulo total derivative, the variation
\delta_{\kappa}
of the Lagrangian form
{\cal L}_{\text{mD}0}

reduces to

\begin{array}[]{c}\begin{split}\mu^{6}\delta_{\kappa}{\cal L}_{\text{mD}0}&=\text{tr}\left[\delta_{\kappa}\mathbb{A}\left(\left[{\mathbb{X}}^{i},{\mathbb{P}}^{i}\right]-4i\{\bm{\Psi}_{q},\bm{\Psi}_{q}\}\right)\right]+{\rm tr}\left(\delta_{\kappa}{\mathbb{P}}^{i}\text{D}{\mathbb{X}}^{i}-\text{D}{\mathbb{P}}^{i}\delta_{\kappa}{\mathbb{X}}^{i}-8i\text{D}{\bm{\Psi}}_{q}\delta_{\kappa}{\bm{\Psi}}_{q}\right)\penalty\ -\\
&-i\frac{2\sqrt{2}}{{\cal M}}(\text{E}^{1q}-\text{E}^{2}_{q})\kappa^{q}{\cal H}+\frac{2}{{\cal M}}\left(1-\frac{{\cal H}}{\mu^{6}}\,\frac{{\cal M}^{\prime}}{{\cal M}}\right)\text{E}^{0}\delta_{\kappa}{\cal H}+\frac{1}{\mu^{6}}\,\frac{{\cal M}^{\prime}}{{\cal M}}\text{d}{\cal K}\delta_{\kappa}{\cal H}\penalty\ +\\
&+\frac{1}{\mu^{6}}\,\frac{1}{2\sqrt{2{\cal M}}}\frac{{\cal M}^{\prime}}{{\cal M}}\,(\text{E}^{1q}-\text{E}_{q}^{2})i\nu_{q}\delta_{\kappa}{\cal H}-\frac{1}{\mu^{6}}\,\frac{{\cal M}^{\prime}}{{\cal M}}\,\delta_{\kappa}{\cal K}\,\text{d}{\cal H}\penalty\ -\\
&-\frac{1}{\mu^{6}}\,\frac{1}{2\sqrt{{\cal M}}}\frac{{\cal M}^{\prime}}{{\cal M}}\,\kappa^{q}i\nu_{q}\,\text{d}{\cal H}-\frac{1}{\sqrt{{\cal M}}}\kappa^{q}i\text{D}\nu_{q}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{2{\cal M}}}(E^{1q}-E_{q}^{2})\,i\delta_{\kappa}\nu_{q}\penalty\ .\end{split}\end{array}

(D.10)

The worldline supersymmetry transformation rules of the matrix fields can be found by requiring this variation to vanish.
As this calculation is a bit subtle, we present below some details.

D.2.1

Worldline supersymmetry transformations of the matrix matter fields

To find the supersymmetry transformation leaving invariant
S_{\text{mD}0}=\int_{\mathcal{W}^{1}}{\cal L}_{\text{mD}0}
, i.e. obeying
\delta_{\kappa}{\cal L}_{\text{mD}0}=0
(modulo total derivative), we have to set equal to zero the coefficients for all the independent 1-forms in (
D.10
). Requiring to vanish the terms proportional to D
{\mathbb{P}}^{i}
, D
{\mathbb{X}}^{i}
and D
{\bm{\Psi}}_{q}
, we find the set of
equations
for the worldline supersymmetry transformations of the matrix “matter” fields of the form of relations (
5.39
), (
5.40
) and (
5.41
). Let us stress that, at this stage, these are equations because their r.h.s.-s contain
\Delta_{\kappa}{\cal K}
from and
\delta_{\kappa}{\cal H}
which, in their turn, are expressed in terms of
\delta_{\kappa}{\mathbb{X}}^{i}
,
\delta_{\kappa}{\mathbb{P}}^{i}
and
\delta_{\kappa}{\bm{\Psi}}_{q}
.

To solve these equations it is convenient to compute formally the variations of composite quantities

\delta_{\kappa}{\cal H}
and
\Delta_{\kappa}{\cal K}
with (
5.39
)-(
5.41
).
On this way we find the following equations

\Delta_{\kappa}{\cal K}=\frac{1}{2\sqrt{{\cal M}}}{\rm tr}\left(4i(\kappa\gamma^{i}\bm{\Psi}){\mathbb{P}}^{i}+{5\over 2}(\kappa\gamma^{ij}\bm{\Psi})[{\mathbb{X}}^{i},{\mathbb{X}}^{j}]\right)-\frac{1}{\mu^{6}}\,\frac{{\cal M}^{\prime}}{{\cal M}}\,\Delta_{\kappa}{\cal K}\,{\mathfrak{H}}\penalty\ ,

(D.11)

\delta_{\kappa}{\cal H}=\frac{1}{2\sqrt{{\cal M}}}{\rm tr}\left(\kappa^{q}{\bm{\Psi}}_{q}\left([{\mathbb{X}}^{i},{\mathbb{P}}^{i}]-4i\{{\bm{\Psi}}_{p},{\bm{\Psi}}_{p}\}\right)\right)-\frac{1}{\mu^{6}}\,\frac{{\cal M}^{\prime}}{{\cal M}}\,\delta_{\kappa}{\cal H}\,{\mathfrak{H}}\penalty\ ,

(D.12)

where
\mathfrak{H}
is given in Eq. (
5.71
)
63
63

63

To obtain (
D.12
) one has to use the identity

\gamma^{i}_{s(r}\gamma^{i}_{pq)}\equiv\delta_{s(r}\delta_{pq)}
and also notice that

{\rm tr}({\bm{\Psi}}_{r}\{{\bm{\Psi}}_{p},{\bm{\Psi}}_{q}\})={\rm tr}({\bm{\Psi}}_{(r}\{{\bm{\Psi}}_{p},{\bm{\Psi}}_{q)}\})
is completely symmetric with respect to
(rpq)
indices while
{\rm tr}([(\gamma^{i}{\bm{\Psi}})_{q},{\mathbb{X}}^{i}]\,[(\gamma^{j}{\bm{\Psi}})_{q},{\mathbb{X}}^{j}])=0
vanishes.

. These equations are solved by (
5.44
) and (
5.42
).

Thus, worldline supersymmetry transformations of the matrix matter fields are given by (
5.39
)-(
5.41
) with
(
5.44
) and (
5.42
).

D.2.2

Worldline supersymmetry transformations of the worldvolume gauge field

Taking into account the above results for supersymmetry transformations of the matrix matter fields, we find that the remaining variation of the Lagrangian form (
D.10
) can be written as

\begin{array}[]{c}\begin{split}\mu^{6}\delta_{\kappa}{\cal L}_{\text{mD}0}&={\rm tr}\left[\delta_{\kappa}{\mathbb{A}}\left([{\mathbb{X}}^{i},{\mathbb{P}}^{i}]-4i\{\bm{\Psi}_{q},\bm{\Psi}_{q}\}\right)\right]+\frac{2}{{\cal M}}\left(1-\frac{{\cal H}}{\mu^{6}}\,\frac{{\cal M}^{\prime}}{{\cal M}}\right)\text{E}^{0}\delta_{\kappa}{\cal H}\penalty\ +\\
&+\frac{1}{\sqrt{2{\cal M}}}(\text{E}^{1q}-\text{E}_{q}^{2})\,\left(i\delta_{\kappa}\nu_{q}-\frac{4i}{\sqrt{{\cal M}}}\kappa^{q}\,{\cal H}+\frac{1}{\mu^{6}}\,\frac{1}{2}\frac{{\cal M}^{\prime}}{{\cal M}}\,i\nu_{q}\delta_{\kappa}{\cal H}\right)\penalty\ .\end{split}\end{array}

(D.13)

To proceed further, we calculate
i\delta_{\kappa}\nu_{q}
which reads

\begin{array}[]{l}\begin{split}i\delta_{\kappa}\nu_{q}&=-\frac{1}{\sqrt{{\cal M}}}\,(\kappa\gamma^{i})_{q}\,{\rm tr}\left[{\mathbb{X}}^{i}\,([{\mathbb{X}}^{j},{\mathbb{P}}^{j}]-4i\{{\bm{\Psi}}_{r},{\bm{\Psi}}_{r}\})\right]+\frac{4i}{\sqrt{{\cal M}}}\kappa^{q}\,{\cal H}+\\
&+\frac{1}{\mu^{6}}\,\frac{{\cal M}^{\prime}}{{\cal M}}\,{\rm tr}\left(4i(\gamma^{i}{\bm{\Psi}})_{q}{\mathbb{P}}^{i}+(\gamma^{ij}{\Psi})_{q}[{\mathbb{X}}^{i},{\mathbb{X}}^{j}]\right)\,\delta_{\kappa}{\cal H}-\\
&-\frac{1}{\mu^{6}}\,\frac{{\cal M}^{\prime}}{{\cal M}}\,\,\Delta_{\kappa}{\cal K}\,{\rm tr}\left[{\bm{\Psi}}_{q}([{\mathbb{X}}^{i},{\mathbb{P}}^{i}]-4i\{{\bm{\Psi}}_{q},{\bm{\Psi}}_{q}\})\right]\end{split}\end{array}

(D.14)

and substitute it to (
D.13
) thus arriving at

\begin{array}[]{l}\begin{split}&\mu^{6}\delta_{\kappa}{\cal L}_{\text{mD}0}={\rm tr}\left\{([{\mathbb{X}}^{i},{\mathbb{P}}^{i}]-4i\{{\bm{\Psi}}_{r},{\bm{\Psi}}_{r}\})\,\left[\delta_{\kappa}{\mathbb{A}}+\dfrac{2}{{\cal M}\sqrt{{\cal M}}}\,\text{E}^{0}\,(\kappa^{q}{\bm{\Psi}}_{q})\dfrac{\left(1-\frac{1}{\mu^{6}}\,\frac{{\cal M}^{\prime}}{{\cal M}}{\cal H}\right)}{\left(1+\frac{1}{\mu^{6}}\,\frac{{\cal M}^{\prime}}{{\cal M}}\,{\mathfrak{H}}\right)}\right.\right.\\
&-\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}{\cal M}}\,(\text{E}^{1q}-\text{E}_{q}^{2})(\gamma^{i}\kappa)_{q}\,{\mathbb{X}}^{i}\penalty\ +\\
&\left.\left.+(\text{E}^{1q}-\text{E}_{q}^{2})\,\frac{1}{\mu^{6}}\,\frac{{\cal M}^{\prime}}{2{\cal M}\sqrt{2{\cal M}}}\,\left(-2\Delta_{\kappa}{\cal K}\,{\bm{\Psi}}_{q}+\frac{\kappa^{p}{\bm{\Psi}}_{p}}{\sqrt{{\cal M}}}\;\frac{{\rm tr}\left(4i(\gamma^{i}{\bm{\Psi}})_{q}{\mathbb{P}}^{i}+\frac{5}{2}(\gamma^{ij}\bm{\Psi})_{q}[{\mathbb{X}}^{i},{\mathbb{X}}^{j}]\right)}{\left(1+\frac{1}{\mu^{6}}\,\frac{{\cal M}^{\prime}}{{\cal M}}\,{\mathfrak{H}}\right)}\,\right)\right]\right\}\penalty\ .\end{split}\end{array}

(D.15)

The above expression vanishes if the SU
(N)
gauge field transforms under worldline supersymmetry as

\begin{array}[]{l}\begin{split}\delta_{\kappa}{\mathbb{A}}&=-\frac{2}{{\cal M}\sqrt{{\cal M}}}\,\text{E}^{0}\,(\kappa^{q}{\bm{\Psi}}_{q})\frac{\left(1-\frac{1}{\mu^{6}}\,\frac{{\cal M}^{\prime}}{{\cal M}}{\cal H}\right)}{\left(1+\frac{1}{\mu^{6}}\,\frac{{\cal M}^{\prime}}{{\cal M}}\,{\mathfrak{H}}\right)}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}{\cal M}}\,(\text{E}^{1q}-E_{q}^{2})(\gamma^{i}\kappa)_{q}\,{\mathbb{X}}^{i}\penalty\ +\\
&\penalty\ \\
&+(\text{E}^{1q}-\text{E}_{q}^{2})\,\frac{1}{\mu^{6}}\,\frac{{\cal M}^{\prime}}{2{\cal M}\sqrt{2{\cal M}}}\,\left(2\Delta_{\kappa}{\cal K}\,{\bm{\Psi}}_{q}-\frac{\kappa^{p}{\bm{\Psi}}_{p}}{\sqrt{{\cal M}}}\;\frac{{\rm tr}\left(4i(\gamma^{i}\bm{\Psi})_{q}{\mathbb{P}}^{i}+\frac{5}{2}(\gamma^{ij}\bm{\Psi})_{q}[{\mathbb{X}}^{i},{\mathbb{X}}^{j}]\right)}{\left(1+\frac{1}{\mu^{6}}\,\frac{{\cal M}^{\prime}}{{\cal M}}\,{\mathfrak{H}}\right)}\right)\penalty\ .\end{split}\end{array}

(D.16)

Finally, substituting the expression (
5.44
) in the above result, we obtain Eq. (
5.47
) from the main text.

Appendix E
A non-supersymmetric solution of mD0 equations

As an example of using the correspondence between the relative motion equations of the mD
0
system and 1d SYM to find non-supersymmetric solutions, let us consider the SYM solution employed in
[
194
]
to explore cosmological scenarios within the BFSS matrix model framework
[
195
]
. This approach is based on the ansatz

{\mathbb{X}}^{i}(\tau)=a(\tau){\mathbb{Y}}^{i}\,

(E.1)

where
{\mathbb{Y}}^{i}
are nine constant traceless
N\times N
matrices satisfying

[[{\mathbb{Y}}^{i},{\mathbb{Y}}^{j}],{\mathbb{Y}}^{j}]=16\lambda{\mathbb{Y}}^{i}

(E.2)

for some constant
\lambda
. Our goal is to find a solution of the mD
0
equations of motion with this ansatz. From Eq. (
5.101
), and fixing the gauge
\dot{x}^{{\bf 0}}=1
, we deduce

{\mathbb{P}}^{i}(\tau)=b(\tau){\mathbb{Y}}^{i}

(E.3)

where the function
b(\tau)
should be determined from solving Eq. (
5.101
) with (
E.1
) and (
E.3
).
Then, with the above ansatz

{\cal H}=c\left(b^{2}+\frac{\lambda}{2}a^{4}\right)\;,\qquad{\mathfrak{H}}=2{\cal H}+\frac{3}{32}{\rm tr}([{\mathbb{X}}^{i},{\mathbb{X}}^{i}]^{2})=2{\cal H}-3c\lambda a^{4}=2c\left(b^{2}-\lambda a^{4}\right)\;,\qquad

(E.4)

with

c=\frac{1}{2}{\rm tr}({\mathbb{Y}}^{i}{\mathbb{Y}}^{i})\;,

(E.5)

so that the straightforward approach to (
5.101
) does not look too promising. However, at this stage we can use the equation (
5.66
) stating that on the mass shell

{\cal H}
is constant and conclude from (
E.4
) that

b^{2}=\frac{{\cal H}}{c}-\frac{\lambda}{2}a^{4}\;,\qquad{\mathfrak{H}}=2{\cal H}-\frac{3\lambda}{2}a^{4}\;.\qquad

(E.6)

Following the method of
[
194
]
, we consider a field-dependent redefinition of the time variable (similar to Eq. (
5.104
))

\text{d}t=\text{d}\tau\,\dfrac{2}{\mathcal{M}}\dfrac{\left(1-\dfrac{1}{\mu^{6}}\dfrac{\mathcal{M^{\prime}}}{\mathcal{M}}\mathcal{H}\right)}{\left[1+\dfrac{1}{\mu^{6}}\dfrac{\mathcal{M^{\prime}}}{\mathcal{M}}\left(2{\cal H}-\dfrac{3\lambda}{2}a^{4}(\tau)\right)\right]}\qquad

(E.7)

and obtain in such a way the equations

\frac{\text{d}}{\text{d}t}a=-b\;,\qquad\frac{\text{d}^{2}a}{\text{d}t^{2}}=-\frac{\lambda}{a}^{3}\;.\qquad

(E.8)

Following
[
194
]
, we can now multiply the second equation by
\frac{\text{d}a}{\text{d}t}
and integrate it over
\text{d}t
thus arriving at

\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{\text{d}a}{\text{d}t}\right)^{2}=-\frac{\lambda}{4}a^{4}+k

(E.9)

with some integration constant
k
. Introducing a further time redefinition,

\text{d}\tilde{t}=\text{d}ta(t)

(E.10)

we obtain a Friedmann-like equation

\frac{1}{a^{2}}\left(\frac{\text{d}a}{\text{d}\tilde{t}}\right)^{2}=-\frac{\lambda}{2}+\frac{2k}{a^{4}}\;.

(E.11)

This is the basis for the cosmological interpretation of the matrix model proposed in
[
194
]
.

Although this final time redefinition (
E.10
) is is structurally similar to our earlier (
5.104
) in our model with non-constant
\mathcal{M}
, the complete equation for the change of time variable is much more complicated

\text{d}\tilde{t}=\text{d}\tau\,\cfrac{2a(\tau)}{\mathcal{M}}\dfrac{\left(1-\cfrac{1}{\mu^{6}}\cfrac{\mathcal{M^{\prime}}}{\mathcal{M}}\mathcal{H}\right)}{\left[1+\dfrac{1}{\mu^{6}}\dfrac{\mathcal{M^{\prime}}}{\mathcal{M}}\left(2{\cal H}-\dfrac{3\lambda}{2}a^{4}(\tau)\right)\right]}\;.\qquad

(E.12)

As a result, to find the dynamics in terms of proper time
\tilde{t}
becomes a more challenging task.

Appendix F
Convenient representations for 11D gamma matrices

In this appendix, we summarize two useful representations of the 11-dimensional gamma matrices
\Gamma^{\underline{\mu}}
and the charge conjugation matrix
C_{\underline{\alpha}\underline{\beta}}
relevant for spinor moving frame (Lorentz harmonics) formalism for M
0
-brane and for reduction of its action to 10D which produces D
0
-brane action.

F.1

SO(1,9) covariant representation

The first representation is manifestly covariant under the 10D Lorentz group
\text{SO}(1,9)\subset\text{SO}(1,10)
. In it

\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \Gamma^{\mu}_{\underline{\alpha}\underline{\beta}}=\left(\begin{matrix}\sigma^{\mu}_{\alpha\beta}&0\cr 0&\tilde{\sigma}{}^{\mu\alpha\beta}\end{matrix}\right)\;,\qquad\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \Gamma^{*}_{\underline{\alpha}\underline{\beta}}=\left(\begin{matrix}0&-\delta_{\alpha}{}^{\beta}\cr-\delta_{\beta}{}^{\alpha}&0\end{matrix}\right)\;,\qquad

(F.1)

\tilde{\Gamma}^{\mu\underline{\alpha}\underline{\beta}}=\left(\begin{matrix}\tilde{\sigma}{}^{\mu\alpha\beta}&0\cr 0&\sigma^{\mu}_{\alpha\beta}\end{matrix}\right)\;,\qquad\tilde{\Gamma}^{*\underline{\alpha}\underline{\beta}}=\left(\begin{matrix}0&\delta_{\beta}{}^{\alpha}\cr\delta_{\alpha}{}^{\beta}&0\end{matrix}\right)\;,\qquad

(F.2)

where
\mu=0,\ldots,9
is a 10D vector index,
\underline{\alpha},\underline{\beta}=1,\ldots,32
and
\alpha,\beta=1,\ldots,16
label 11D Majorana and 10D Majorana-Weyl spinor indices, respectively.

In this representation, 11D charge conjugation matrix takes the form

C_{\underline{\alpha}\underline{\beta}}=i\,\left(\begin{matrix}0&\delta_{\alpha}{}^{\beta}\cr-\delta_{\beta}{}^{\alpha}&0\end{matrix}\right)\;,\qquad C^{\underline{\alpha}\underline{\beta}}=i\,\left(\begin{matrix}0&\delta_{\beta}{}^{\alpha}\cr-\delta_{\alpha}{}^{\beta}&0\end{matrix}\right)\;,\qquad

(F.3)

and it is easy to check that

\tilde{\Gamma}^{\underline{\mu}}=C{\Gamma}^{\underline{\mu}}C\;.

(F.4)

F.2

SO(1,1)
\otimes
SO(9) covariant representation

The second representation is adapted to the light-cone decomposition of 11D spacetime and is covariant under the subgroup
\text{SO}(1,1)\otimes\text{SO}(9)
. It is particularly useful in spinor moving frame formulation of massless 11D superparticle:

\displaystyle(\Gamma^{\#})_{\underline{\alpha}\underline{\beta}}=\left(\begin{matrix}2\delta_{pq}&0\cr 0&0\end{matrix}\right)=\tilde{\Gamma}{}^{=\underline{\alpha}\underline{\beta}}\;,\qquad(\Gamma^{=})_{\underline{\alpha}\underline{\beta}}=\left(\begin{matrix}0&0\cr 0&2\delta_{pq}\end{matrix}\right)=\tilde{\Gamma}{}^{\#\underline{\alpha}\underline{\beta}}\;,\qquad

(F.5)

\displaystyle(\Gamma^{i})_{\underline{\alpha}\underline{\beta}}=\left(\begin{matrix}0&\gamma^{i}_{pq}\cr\gamma^{i}_{pq}&0\end{matrix}\right)=-\tilde{\Gamma}{}^{i\underline{\alpha}\underline{\beta}}\;,

(F.6)

where
i=1,\dots,9
are spatial SO(9) vector indices and
p,q=1,\dots,16
are SO(9) spinor indices. The charge conjugation matrix in this representation is

\displaystyle C_{\underline{\alpha}\underline{\beta}}=-C_{\underline{\beta}\underline{\alpha}}=\left(\begin{matrix}0&i\delta_{pq}\cr-i\delta_{pq}&0\cr\end{matrix}\right)=(C^{-1}){}^{\underline{\alpha}\underline{\beta}}=:C^{\underline{\alpha}\underline{\beta}}\;.\qquad

(F.7)

Appendix G
Useful Poisson bracket relations for matrix fields, constraints and currents of 3D mD0

In this appendix we collect the Poisson bracket relations for matrix variables, SU
(N)
constraints and currents of 3D mD
0
system.

Let us begin by presenting the Poisson brackets of the Gauss law constraint

\mathbb{G}
(
7.78
)
with the matrix variables. It is convenient to encode these in the brackets of the trace of the product of
\mathbb{G}
with some “reference” traceless matrix
\mathbb{Y}
, i.e. of
\text{tr}(\mathbb{Y}\mathbb{G})=\mathbb{Y}_{i}^{j}\mathbb{G}^{i}_{j}
. In such a way we arrive at simple and transparent expressions

\begin{array}[]{ccc}[\text{tr}(\mathbb{Y}\mathbb{G}),\mathbb{Z}]_{\text{PB}}=[\mathbb{Z},\mathbb{Y}]\penalty\ ,&&[\text{tr}(\mathbb{Y}\mathbb{G}),\bar{\mathbb{Z}}]_{\text{PB}}=[\bar{\mathbb{Z}},\mathbb{Y}]\penalty\ ,\end{array}

(G.1)

\begin{array}[]{ccc}[\text{tr}(\mathbb{Y}\mathbb{G}),\mathbb{P}]_{\text{PB}}=[\mathbb{P},\mathbb{Y}]\penalty\ ,&&[\text{tr}(\mathbb{Y}\mathbb{G}),\bar{\mathbb{P}}]_{\text{PB}}=[\bar{\mathbb{P}},\mathbb{Y}]\penalty\ ,\end{array}

(G.2)

\begin{array}[]{ccc}[\text{tr}(\mathbb{Y}\mathbb{G}),{\bm{\Psi}}]_{\text{PB}}=[{\bm{\Psi}},\mathbb{Y}]\penalty\ ,&&[\text{tr}(\mathbb{Y}\mathbb{G}),\bar{{\bm{\Psi}}}]_{\text{PB}}=[\bar{{\bm{\Psi}}},\mathbb{Y}]\end{array}

(G.3)

which clearly represent the infinitesimal SU
(N)
transformations.

These relations allow, in particular, to obtain the
\mathfrak{su}(N)
algebra (
7.83
) generated by the Gauss constraints

[\text{tr}(\mathbb{Y}\mathbb{G}),\text{tr}(\mathbb{Y}^{\prime}\mathbb{G})]_{\text{PB}}=\text{tr}\left([\mathbb{Y},\mathbb{Y}^{\prime}]\mathbb{G}\right)\penalty\ .

(G.4)

It is also easy to check that the Poisson brackets of the Gauss law with different currents that appear in our constraints,

\begin{array}[]{ccccc}{\nu}:=\text{tr}({\bm{\Psi}}\mathbb{P}+\bar{{\bm{\Psi}}}[\mathbb{Z},\bar{\mathbb{Z}}])\penalty\ ,&&{\bar{\nu}}:=\text{tr}(\bar{{\bm{\Psi}}}\bar{\mathbb{P}}+{\bm{\Psi}}[\mathbb{Z},\bar{\mathbb{Z}}])\penalty\ ,&&\mathcal{B}:=\text{tr}\left(\bar{\mathbb{P}}\mathbb{Z}-\mathbb{P}\bar{\mathbb{Z}}+\dfrac{i}{8}{{\bm{\Psi}}}\bar{{\bm{\Psi}}}\right),\end{array}

(G.5)

and

\begin{array}[]{ccc}\mathcal{H}={\rm tr}\left({\mathbb{P}}\bar{\mathbb{P}}+[{\mathbb{Z}},\bar{\mathbb{Z}}]^{2}-\dfrac{i}{2}{\mathbb{Z}}{{\bm{\Psi}}}{{\bm{\Psi}}}+\dfrac{i}{2}\bar{\mathbb{Z}}\bar{{\bm{\Psi}}}\bar{{\bm{\Psi}}}\right)\penalty\ ,\end{array}

(G.6)

vanish,

\displaystyle[\text{tr}(\mathbb{Y}\mathbb{G}),\nu]_{\text{PB}}=0\penalty\ ,\qquad[\text{tr}(\mathbb{Y}\mathbb{G}),\bar{\nu}]_{\text{PB}}=0\penalty\ ,\qquad[\text{tr}(\mathbb{Y}\mathbb{G}),\mathcal{B}]_{\text{PB}}=0\penalty\ ,\qquad[\text{tr}(\mathbb{Y}\mathbb{G}),\mathcal{H}]_{\text{PB}}=0\penalty\ .\qquad

(G.7)

This is just the reflection of SU
(N)
invariance of these objects.

The Poisson brackets of the fermionic and bosonic currents (
G.5
) among themselves and with the “relative motion Hamiltonian” (
G.6
) are

\begin{array}[]{ccccc}\{\nu,\nu\}_{\text{PB}}=-8i(\mu^{6})^{2}{\rm tr}({\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{G}})\penalty\ ,&&\{\bar{\nu},\bar{\nu}\}_{\text{PB}}=8i(\mu^{6})^{2}{\rm tr}(\bar{{\mathbb{Z}}}{\mathbb{G}})\penalty\ ,&&\{\nu,\bar{\nu}\}_{\text{PB}}=-4i\mu^{6}\mathcal{H}\penalty\ ,\\
\\
{}[\nu,\mathcal{B}]_{\text{PB}}=\dfrac{1}{2}\mu^{6}\nu,&&[\bar{\nu},\mathcal{B}]_{\text{PB}}=-\dfrac{1}{2}\mu^{6}\bar{\nu}\penalty\ ,&&\end{array}

(G.8)

and

\begin{array}[]{ccccc}[\nu,\mathcal{H}]_{\text{PB}}=(\mu^{6})^{2}{\rm tr}(\bar{{\bm{\Psi}}}{\mathbb{G}})\penalty\ ,&&[\bar{\nu},\mathcal{H}]_{\text{PB}}=-(\mu^{6})^{2}{\rm tr}({\bm{\Psi}}{\mathbb{G}})\penalty\ ,&&[\mathcal{B},\mathcal{H}]_{\text{PB}}=0\penalty\ .\\
\end{array}

(G.9)

In terms of renormalized currents and matrix fields (
7.122
), (
7.137
) and (
7.159
), the above relations simplify to

\begin{array}[]{ccccc}\{\tilde{\nu},\tilde{\nu}\}_{\text{PB}}=-{2i}{\rm tr}({\mathbb{Z}}\tilde{{\mathbb{G}}})\penalty\ ,&&\{\bar{\tilde{\nu}},\bar{\tilde{\nu}}\}_{\text{PB}}=2i{\rm tr}(\bar{{\mathbb{Z}}}\tilde{{\mathbb{G}}})\penalty\ ,&&\{\tilde{\nu},\bar{\tilde{\nu}}\}_{\text{PB}}=-i\tilde{\mathcal{H}}\penalty\ ,\\
\\
{}[\tilde{\nu},\tilde{\mathcal{B}}]_{\text{PB}}=\dfrac{1}{2}\tilde{\nu},&&[\bar{\tilde{\nu}},\tilde{\mathcal{B}}]_{\text{PB}}=-\dfrac{1}{2}\bar{\tilde{\nu}}\penalty\ ,&&[\tilde{\mathcal{B}},\tilde{\mathcal{B}}]_{\text{PB}}\equiv 0\penalty\ ,\\
\\
{}[\tilde{\nu},\tilde{\mathcal{H}}]_{\text{PB}}=\dfrac{1}{2}{\rm tr}(\tilde{\bar{{\bm{\Psi}}}}\tilde{{\mathbb{G}}})\penalty\ ,&&[\bar{\tilde{\nu}},\tilde{\mathcal{H}}]_{\text{PB}}=-\dfrac{1}{2}{\rm tr}(\tilde{{{\bm{\Psi}}}}\tilde{{\mathbb{G}}})\penalty\ ,&&[\tilde{\mathcal{B}},\tilde{\mathcal{H}}]_{\text{PB}}=0\penalty\ .\\
\end{array}

(G.10)

The above relations were obtained by using the brackets of currents with matrix fields which are

\begin{array}[]{lllll}[\nu,\mathbb{Z}]_{\text{PB}}=0\penalty\ ,&&[\nu,\mathbb{P}]_{\text{PB}}=0\penalty\ ,&&\{\nu,{\bm{\Psi}}\}_{\text{PB}}=-{4i\mu^{6}[\mathbb{Z},\bar{\mathbb{Z}}]}\penalty\ ,\\
\\
{}[\nu,\bar{\mathbb{Z}}]_{\text{PB}}=-{\mu^{6}{\bm{\Psi}}}\penalty\ ,&&[\nu,\bar{\mathbb{P}}]_{\text{PB}}=0\penalty\ ,&&\{\nu,\bar{{\bm{\Psi}}}\}_{\text{PB}}=-4i\mu^{6}\mathbb{P},\\
\\
{}[\bar{\nu},\mathbb{Z}]_{\text{PB}}=-{\mu^{6}\bar{{\bm{\Psi}}}}\penalty\ ,&&[\bar{\nu},\mathbb{P}]_{\text{PB}}=0\penalty\ ,&&\{\bar{\nu},{\bm{\Psi}}\}_{\text{PB}}=-4i\mu^{6}\bar{\mathbb{P}}\penalty\ ,\\
\\
{}[\bar{\nu},\bar{\mathbb{Z}}]_{\text{PB}}=0\penalty\ ,&&[\bar{\nu},\bar{\mathbb{P}}]_{\text{PB}}=0\penalty\ ,&&\{\bar{\nu},\bar{{\bm{\Psi}}}\}_{\text{PB}}={-4i\mu^{6}[\mathbb{Z},\bar{\mathbb{Z}}]}\penalty\ ,\\
\\
{}[\mathcal{B},\mathbb{Z}]_{\text{PB}}=-\mu^{6}\mathbb{Z}\penalty\ ,&&[\mathcal{B},\mathbb{P}]_{\text{PB}}=-\mu^{6}\mathbb{P}\penalty\ ,&&[\mathcal{B},{\bm{\Psi}}]_{\text{PB}}=\;\dfrac{1}{2}\mu^{6}{\bm{\Psi}}\penalty\ ,\\
\\
{}[\mathcal{B},\bar{\mathbb{Z}}]_{\text{PB}}=\mu^{6}\bar{\mathbb{Z}}\penalty\ ,&&[\mathcal{B},\bar{\mathbb{P}}]_{\text{PB}}=\mu^{6}\bar{\mathbb{P}}\penalty\ ,&&[\mathcal{B},\bar{{\bm{\Psi}}}]_{\text{PB}}=-\dfrac{1}{2}\mu^{6}\bar{{\bm{\Psi}}}\penalty\ .\end{array}

(G.11)

Appendix H
Problems with Gupta-Bleuler quantization scheme for massive particle in moving frame formulation

Let us consider the system of first class constraints (
7.99
), (
7.102
) and second class constraints (
7.103
), (
7.104
) and set to zero all fermionic and matrix fields. Under these conditions, the system of constraints describes, within in the frame of Hamiltonian approach, just a massive bosonic particle in its spinor moving frame formulation.

In this appendix we show that, somewhat unexpectedly, the quantization of such a simple system by the Gupta-Bleuler method in its canonical form fails
64
64

64

Of course, the quantization of the same system in the frame of standard formulation, using the bosonic limit of the action (
7.8
), can be easily performed and gives an expected result: the theory of free scalar field obeying the Klein-Gordon equation. However, our interest lies in quantization of this simple system in its moving frame formulation, since this arises as a pure bosonic limit of our 3D mD
0
system when we set to zero all the matrix fields.
.

Imposing the quantum versions of the first class constraints (
7.99
), (
7.102
) and two of second class constraints (
7.103
), (
7.104
) which commute and are not related by Hermitian conjugation among themselves, and choosing a coordinate representation for the state vector,
\Xi_{0}=\Xi_{0}(x^{a},\bar{w},w)
, we arrive at the following system of differential equations

\displaystyle(iu^{(0)a}\partial_{a}+m)\Xi_{0}=0\;,\qquad

(H.1)

\displaystyle\left({\mathbb{D}}^{(0)}-q\right)\Xi_{0}=0\;,\qquad

(H.2)

\displaystyle\bar{{\mathbb{D}}}\Xi_{0}=0\;,\qquad

(H.3)

\displaystyle\bar{u}{}^{a}\partial_{a}\Xi_{0}=0\;.\qquad

(H.4)

The
x^{a}
-dependence of the state vector is fixed by Eqs. (
H.1
) and (
H.4
) to be

\Xi_{0}=e^{imx^{a}{u}_{a}^{(0)}}\,\chi_{0}(x^{a}\bar{u}_{a},\bar{w}_{\alpha},w_{\alpha})\;.\qquad

(H.5)

Substituting this into (
H.3
), we find that
\chi_{0}(x^{a}\bar{u}_{a},\bar{w}_{\alpha},w_{\alpha})
satisfies

\bar{{\mathbb{D}}}\chi_{0}(x^{a}\bar{u}_{a},\bar{w}_{\alpha},w_{\alpha}):=\bar{w}_{\alpha}\frac{\partial}{\partial{w}_{\alpha}}\chi_{0}(x^{a}\bar{u}_{a},\bar{w}_{\alpha},w_{\alpha})\;=-imx^{a}\bar{u}_{a}\,\chi_{0}(x^{a}\bar{u}_{a},\bar{w}_{\alpha},w_{\alpha})\;.

(H.6)

The second form of the l.h.s. of this equation makes manifest that its nontrivial solution, if existed, would be
\chi_{0}=e^{-imx^{a}{u}_{a}^{(0)}}\tilde{\chi}_{0}(x^{a}\bar{u}_{a},\bar{w}_{\alpha},w_{\alpha})
, which contradicts the fact that, in (
H.5
),
\chi_{0}
is independent of
x^{a}u_{a}^{(0)}
.

This example (actually the only presently known to the authors) indicates that the selfconsistency of the Gupta-Bleuler quantization method is not guaranteed and thus its should be used with precaution.

Curiously, if we modify the above procedure by imposing, besides (
H.1
) and (
H.2
), the complex (Hermitian) conjugate but not canonically conjugate pair of second class constraints (
7.103
), which commute among themselves, we obtain the system

\displaystyle(iu^{(0)a}\partial_{a}+m)\Xi_{0}=0\;,\qquad

(H.7)

\displaystyle\left({\mathbb{D}}^{(0)}-q\right)\Xi_{0}=0\;,\qquad

(H.8)

\displaystyle u^{a}\partial_{a}\Xi_{0}=0\;,\qquad

(H.9)

\displaystyle\bar{u}{}^{a}\partial_{a}\Xi_{0}=0\qquad\;.

(H.10)

This system does have a nontrivial solution which is equivalent to the solution of the massive Klein-Gordon equation.

While this modification falls outside the standard Gupta-Bleuler prescription, as we show in the main text and also in Appendix
I
, its result is equivalent to the quantization with the method of Dirac brackets, which is further simplified and reduced to the explicit resolution of the constraints in the analytical basis. Such Dirac brackets/analytical basis quantization gives a consistent field theory with a nontrivial solution which, in the simplest case considered here, reproduces the result of quantization in the standard formulation of the massive particle.

Appendix I
Hamiltonian formalism of single 3D D0-brane in the so-called analytical basis

In this appendix we provide the details of the Hamiltonian formalism for a single D
0
-brane in the analytical basis.

The Lagrangian of this 3D counterpart of the D
0
-brane in the spinor moving frame formulation (
cf.
(
7.8
)) reads

\mathcal{L}_{\text{D}0}=\text{d}\tau\mathcal{L}_{\tau\text{D}0}=\,m{\rm E}^{0}+m(\text{d}\theta^{\alpha}\bar{\theta}_{\alpha}-\text{d}\bar{\theta}^{\alpha}\theta_{\alpha})

(I.1)

where
{\rm E}^{(0)}
is defined in (
C.5
) with (
7.10
). The dynamical variables in this Lagrangian 1-form are coordinate functions that parametrize the embedding of the worldline in flat superspace

\begin{array}[]{ccccccc}\mathcal{W}^{1}\subset\Sigma^{\left(3\left.\right|4\right)}:&&x^{a}=x^{a}(\tau),&&\theta^{\alpha}=\theta^{\alpha}(\tau),&&\bar{\theta}^{\alpha}=\bar{\theta}^{\alpha}(\tau)\end{array}\penalty\

(I.2)

as well as the spinor frame variables
w_{\alpha}(\tau)
and its complex conjugate
\bar{w}_{\alpha}(\tau)
, satisfying (
7.13
). In the terminology of
[
144
]
, originating in the seminal works on Harmonic superspaces
[
152
,
177
,
146
,
147
]
, these spinor frame variables can be referred to as Lorentz harmonics, reflecting the fact that they parametrize the double cover of the 3D Lorentz group SL
(2,{\mathbb{R}})
. Upon imposing invariance under U
(1)
gauge transformations acting on
w
and
\bar{w}
, they serve as homogeneous coordinates on the coset SL
(2,{\mathbb{R}})/\text{U}(1)
. Furthermore, the configuration space of this dynamical system (the target space of the spinor moving frame formulation of the 3D D
0
-brane) can be called, following the same terminology,
Lorentz harmonic superspace
which we denoted
\Sigma^{(3+3|4)}
. This is the superspace with coordinates

\Sigma^{(3+3|4)}=\{(x^{a},\theta^{\alpha},\bar{\theta}^{\alpha},w_{\alpha},\bar{w}_{\alpha})\}=:\{(Z^{M},w_{\alpha},\bar{w}_{\alpha})\}\;,\qquad\bar{w}^{\alpha}{w}_{\alpha}=i\;.

(I.3)

In the spinor moving frame approach we are considering D
0
-brane as a particle moving in this extended and enlarged
\text{D}=3
superspace. Here “extended” refers to
{\cal N}=2
supersymmetry and “enlarged” reflects the presence of additional bosonic spinor (spinor moving frame or Lorentz harmonic) coordinates.

This perspective is convenient, in particular, because it suggests the possibility to choose an alternative coordinate basis of the Lorentz harmonic superspace,
the analytical coordinate basis
which is given by

\Sigma^{(3+3|4)}=\{({\rm x}{}^{(0)},{\rm x}_{A},\bar{{\rm x}}_{A}\;,\theta^{w},{\theta}^{\bar{w}},\bar{\theta}^{{w}},\bar{\theta}^{\bar{w}};w_{\alpha},\bar{w}_{\alpha})\}=:\{({\rm Z}{}^{(M)}_{An},\;w_{\alpha},\bar{w}_{\alpha})\}\;,\qquad\bar{w}^{\alpha}{w}_{\alpha}=i\;

(I.4)

where (see
[
144
,
146
,
147
]
)

\begin{array}[]{c}{\rm x}^{(0)}:=x^{a}u_{a}^{(0)}\;,\\
\qquad\framebox{${\rm x}_{A}:={\rm x}-2i\theta^{w}\bar{\theta}^{{w}}$}=x^{a}u_{a}-2i\theta^{w}\bar{\theta}^{{w}}\;,\qquad\framebox{$\bar{{\rm x}}_{A}:=\bar{{\rm x}}+2i{\theta}^{\bar{w}}\bar{\theta}^{\bar{w}}$}=x^{a}\bar{u}_{a}+2i{\theta}^{\bar{w}}\bar{\theta}^{\bar{w}}\;,\end{array}

(I.5)

\theta^{w}:=\theta^{\alpha}w_{\alpha}\penalty\ ,\qquad\theta^{\bar{w}}:=\theta^{\alpha}\bar{w}_{\alpha}\penalty\ ,

(I.6)

\bar{\theta}^{w}:=\bar{\theta}^{\alpha}w_{\alpha}\penalty\ ,\qquad\bar{\theta}^{\bar{w}}:=\bar{\theta}^{\alpha}\bar{w}_{\alpha}\penalty\ .\qquad

(I.7)

The definition of
{\rm x}_{A}=(\bar{{\rm x}}_{A})^{*}
in (
I.5
) is designed in such a way that, in the analytical basis, the Lagrangian form (
I.1
) simplifies to

\begin{array}[]{l}\begin{split}\mathcal{L}_{\text{D}0}&=m\text{d}{\rm x}^{(0)}-imf\;\bar{{\rm x}}_{A}+im\bar{f}\;{\rm x}_{A}-4ma\theta^{\bar{w}}\bar{\theta}^{w}-2im(\text{d}\theta^{\bar{w}}\bar{\theta}^{w}+\text{d}\bar{\theta}^{w}\theta^{\bar{w}})=\\
&=m\text{d}{\rm x}^{(0)}-2im(\text{d}\theta^{\bar{w}}-ia\theta^{\bar{w}})\,\bar{\theta}^{w}-2im(\text{d}\bar{\theta}^{w}+ia\bar{\theta}^{w})\,\theta^{\bar{w}}-imf\;\bar{{\rm x}}_{A}+im\bar{f}\;{\rm x}_{A}\;.\end{split}\end{array}

(I.8)

In the main text we also needed explicit expressions for the kinetic and WZ terms in the single 3D D
0
action,

\begin{array}[]{l}\begin{split}{\rm E}^{0}&=\text{d}{\rm x}^{(0)}-i(\text{d}\theta^{{w}}+ia\theta^{{w}})\,\bar{\theta}^{\bar{w}}-i(\text{d}\theta^{\bar{w}}-i(\text{d}\bar{\theta}^{w}+ia\bar{\theta}^{w})\theta^{\bar{w}}-ia\theta^{\bar{w}})\,\bar{\theta}^{w}-\\
&-i(\text{d}\bar{\theta}^{\bar{w}}-ia\bar{\theta}^{\bar{w}})\theta^{w}+if\;\bar{{\rm x}}-i\bar{f}\;{\rm x}\;,\end{split}\end{array}

(I.9)

\begin{array}[]{l}\begin{split}\text{d}\theta^{\alpha}\bar{\theta}_{\alpha}-\text{d}\bar{\theta}^{\alpha}{\theta}_{\alpha}&=2f\;\theta^{\bar{w}}\bar{\theta}^{{\bar{w}}}+2\bar{f}\;\theta^{{w}}\bar{\theta}^{{w}}+i(\text{d}\theta^{{w}}+ia\theta^{{w}})\,\bar{\theta}^{\bar{w}}-i(\text{d}\theta^{\bar{w}}-ia\theta^{\bar{w}})\,\bar{\theta}^{w}-\\
&-i(\text{d}\bar{\theta}^{w}+ia\bar{\theta}^{w})\theta^{\bar{w}}+i(\text{d}\bar{\theta}^{\bar{w}}-ia\bar{\theta}^{\bar{w}})\theta^{w}\;.\end{split}\end{array}

(I.10)

The canonical Hamiltonian
H_{0}
, expressed in terms of the analytical basis coordinates, is defined by

\text{d}\tau H_{0}=\text{d}\text{x}^{(0)}p^{(0)}+\text{d}{\rm x}_{A}\bar{p}+\text{d}\bar{\text{x}}_{A}p+\text{d}\theta^{w}\Pi^{\theta}_{w}+\text{d}\theta^{\bar{w}}\Pi^{\theta}_{\bar{w}}+\text{d}\bar{\theta}^{w}\bar{\Pi}^{\bar{\theta}}_{w}+\text{d}\bar{\theta}^{\bar{w}}\bar{\Pi}^{\bar{\theta}}_{\bar{w}}+ia\tilde{\mathfrak{d}}^{(0)}+if\bar{\tilde{\mathfrak{d}}}-i\bar{f}\tilde{\mathfrak{d}}-\mathcal{L}_{\text{D}0}\penalty\ .

(I.11)

The momenta conjugate to the coordinate functions, i.e. having the nonvanishing Poisson brackets

\begin{array}[]{ccccc}\left[p^{(0)},\text{x}^{(0)}\right]_{\text{PB}}=-1\penalty\ ,&&\left[p,\bar{{\rm x}}_{A}\right]_{\text{PB}}=-1\penalty\ ,&&\left[\bar{p},{\rm x}_{A}\right]_{\text{PB}}=-1\penalty\ ,\end{array}

(I.12)

are defined by (
7.125
),

\begin{array}[]{ccccc}p^{(0)}:=u^{a(0)}p_{a}\penalty\ ,&&p:=-\dfrac{1}{2}u^{a}p_{a}\penalty\ ,&&\bar{p}:=-\dfrac{1}{2}\bar{u}^{a}p_{a}\penalty\ .\end{array}

(I.13)

Similarly, the momenta conjugate to the fermionic coordinate functions satisfy

\begin{array}[]{ccccccc}{}\{\Pi^{\theta}_{w},\theta^{w}\}_{\text{PB}}=-1\penalty\ ,&&\{\Pi^{\theta}_{\bar{w}},\theta^{\bar{w}}\}_{\text{PB}}=-1\penalty\ ,&&\{\bar{\Pi}^{\bar{\theta}}_{w},\bar{\theta}^{w}\}_{\text{PB}}=-1\penalty\ ,&&\{\bar{\Pi}^{\bar{\theta}}_{\bar{w}},\bar{\theta}^{\bar{w}}\}_{\text{PB}}=-1\penalty\ .\end{array}

(I.14)

and are related to the ones of the central basis by (
7.128
)

\begin{array}[]{ccc}\Pi^{\theta}_{w}:=-i\bar{w}^{\alpha}\Pi_{\alpha}+2i\bar{\theta}^{w}\bar{p}\penalty\ ,&&\Pi^{\theta}_{\bar{w}}:=iw^{\alpha}\Pi_{\alpha}-2i\bar{\theta}^{\bar{w}}p\penalty\ ,\\
\\
\bar{\Pi}^{\bar{\theta}}_{w}:=-i\bar{w}^{\alpha}\bar{\Pi}_{\alpha}-2i\theta^{w}\bar{p}\penalty\ ,&&\bar{\Pi}^{\bar{\theta}}_{\bar{w}}:=iw^{\alpha}\bar{\Pi}_{\alpha}+2i\theta^{\bar{w}}p\penalty\ .\end{array}

(I.15)

The covariant momenta in the analytical basis are related to those in the central basis as

\begin{array}[]{lll}&&\mathfrak{d}:=\tilde{\mathfrak{d}}+(\text{x}_{A}+2i\theta^{w}\bar{\theta}^{\bar{w}})p^{(0)}+2(\text{x}^{(0)}+i\theta^{w}\bar{\theta}^{\bar{w}}+i\theta^{\bar{w}}\bar{\theta}^{{w}})p+\theta^{w}\Pi_{\bar{w}}^{\theta}+\bar{\theta}^{w}\bar{\Pi}_{\bar{w}}^{\bar{\theta}}\penalty\ ,\\
\\
&&\bar{\mathfrak{d}}:=\bar{\tilde{\mathfrak{d}}}+(\bar{\text{x}}_{A}-2i{\theta}^{\bar{w}}\bar{\theta}^{\bar{w}})p^{(0)}+2(\text{x}^{(0)}-i{\theta}^{{w}}\bar{\theta}^{\bar{w}}-i{\theta}^{\bar{w}}\bar{\theta}^{{w}})\bar{p}+\theta^{\bar{w}}\Pi_{w}^{\theta}+\bar{\theta}^{\bar{w}}\bar{\Pi}_{w}^{\bar{\theta}}\penalty\ ,\\
\\
&&{\mathfrak{d}}^{(0)}:=\tilde{\mathfrak{d}}^{(0)}-2\text{x}_{A}\bar{p}+2\bar{\text{x}}_{A}p+\theta^{\bar{w}}\Pi_{\bar{w}}^{\theta}+\bar{\theta}^{\bar{w}}\bar{\Pi}_{\bar{w}}^{\bar{\theta}}-\theta^{w}\Pi^{\theta}_{w}-\bar{\theta}^{w}\bar{\Pi}^{\bar{\theta}}_{w}\penalty\ .\end{array}

(I.16)

This implies that

\begin{array}[]{lll}{}[\tilde{\mathfrak{d}}^{(0)},{\rm Z}_{An}^{(M)}]_{\text{PB}}=0\;,\qquad{}[\tilde{\mathfrak{d}},{\rm Z}_{An}^{(M)}]_{\text{PB}}=0\;,\qquad{}[\bar{\tilde{\mathfrak{d}}},{\rm Z}_{An}^{(M)}]_{\text{PB}}=0\;,\end{array}

(I.17)

while

\begin{array}[]{lll}{}[{\mathfrak{d}}^{(0)},Z^{M}]_{\text{PB}}=0\;,\qquad{}[{\mathfrak{d}},Z^{M}]_{\text{PB}}=0\;,\qquad{}[\bar{{\mathfrak{d}}},Z^{M}]_{\text{PB}}=0\;.\end{array}

(I.18)

The calculation of all the canonical and covariant momenta results in the (primary) constraints

\displaystyle\Phi^{(0)}:=p^{(0)}-m\approx 0\;,\qquad

\displaystyle\qquad\bar{\Phi}:=\bar{p}\approx 0\;,\qquad

\displaystyle\Phi:=p\approx 0\;,\qquad

(I.19)

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathfrak{d}}^{(0)}-4im\theta^{\bar{w}}\bar{\theta}^{w}\approx 0\penalty\ ,\qquad

\displaystyle\qquad\tilde{\mathfrak{d}}+m{\rm x}_{A}\approx 0\penalty\ ,\qquad

\displaystyle\bar{\tilde{\mathfrak{d}}}+m\bar{{\rm x}}_{A}\approx 0\penalty\ ,

(I.20)

\displaystyle d_{w}:=\Pi^{\theta}_{w}\approx 0\penalty\ ,\qquad

\displaystyle\qquad d_{\bar{w}}:=\Pi^{\theta}_{\bar{w}}+2im\bar{\theta}^{w}\approx 0\penalty\ ,\qquad

(I.21)

\displaystyle\bar{d}_{\bar{w}}:=\bar{\Pi}^{\bar{\theta}}_{\bar{w}}\approx 0\penalty\ ,\qquad

\displaystyle\qquad\bar{d}_{w}:=\bar{\Pi}^{\bar{\theta}}_{w}+2im\theta^{\bar{w}}\approx 0\penalty\ .\qquad

(I.22)

We have written these in three columns in such a way that the rows correspond to different sectors of dynamical variables. From the second and third columns one can read the pairs of conjugate second class constraints (which, in the case of bosonic constraints, are explicitly solved), while the first column contains a prototypes of the first class constraints.

The canonical Hamiltonian vanishes on the surface of primary constraints, this is

H_{0}\approx 0\;.

(I.23)

The true first class constraints,

\Phi^{(0)}\approx 0,\qquad d_{w},\approx 0,\qquad\bar{d}_{\bar{w}}\approx 0,\qquad\text{and}\qquad\tilde{\tilde{U}}{}^{(0)}\approx 0,\qquad

(I.24)

are given by the first equations in (
I.19
), (
I.21
), (
I.22
) and by the sum of the first equation in (
I.20
) with certain linear combination of the second class constraints,

\displaystyle\tilde{\tilde{U}}{}^{(0)}=\tilde{\mathfrak{d}}^{(0)}-4im\theta^{\bar{w}}\bar{\theta}^{w}-2\text{x}_{A}\bar{p}+2\bar{\text{x}}_{A}p-\bar{\theta}^{w}\bar{d}_{w}+{\theta}^{\bar{w}}d_{\bar{w}}=\qquad

\displaystyle=\tilde{\mathfrak{d}}^{(0)}-2\text{x}_{A}\bar{p}+2\bar{\text{x}}_{A}p-\bar{\theta}^{w}\bar{\Pi}^{\bar{\theta}}_{w}+{\theta}^{\bar{w}}{\Pi}^{{\theta}}_{\bar{w}}\approx 0.\;

(I.25)

Also the set of second class constraints, in order to put their algebra in the canonical form, should be redefined a bit and written as

{\text{2nd class}}:\qquad\qquad\begin{cases}\bar{\Phi}=\bar{p}\approx 0\cr\tilde{\tilde{\mathfrak{d}}}\approx 0\end{cases}\;,\qquad\begin{cases}{\Phi}={p}\approx 0\cr\bar{\tilde{\tilde{\mathfrak{d}}}}\approx 0\end{cases}\;,\qquad\begin{cases}d_{\bar{w}}\approx 0\cr\bar{d}_{w}\approx 0\end{cases}\;,\qquad

(I.26)

where

\tilde{\tilde{\mathfrak{d}}}:=\tilde{\mathfrak{d}}+m{\rm x}_{A}+2i{\theta}^{\bar{w}}\bar{\theta}^{w}p\;,\qquad\bar{\tilde{\tilde{\mathfrak{d}}}}:=\bar{\tilde{\mathfrak{d}}}+m\bar{{\rm x}}_{A}-2i{\theta}^{\bar{w}}\bar{\theta}^{w}\bar{p}\penalty\ .\qquad

(I.27)

The algebra of the first class constraints (
I.24
) is Abelian (this is to say all the Poisson brackets of the first class constraints vanish). The only nonvanishing brackets between first class constraints and second class constraints appear when the first class constraint is given by the U
(1)
generator (
I.25
),

{}[\tilde{\tilde{U}}{}^{(0)}\,,\,\left(\begin{matrix}\bar{p}\cr\tilde{\tilde{\mathfrak{d}}}\cr{p}\cr\bar{\tilde{\tilde{\mathfrak{d}}}}\cr d_{\bar{w}}\cr\bar{d}_{w}\end{matrix}\right)]_{{\text{PB}}}=\,\left(\begin{matrix}-2\bar{p}\cr 2\tilde{\tilde{\mathfrak{d}}}\cr 2{p}\cr-2\bar{\tilde{\tilde{\mathfrak{d}}}}\cr d_{\bar{w}}\cr-\bar{d}_{w}\end{matrix}\right)\,.

(I.28)

The coefficients in the r.h.s. represent the charges of second class constraints with respect to U
(1)
gauge symmetry.

Finally, the nonvanishing brackets of the second class constraints are given by

{}[\bar{p}\,,\,\tilde{\tilde{\mathfrak{d}}}]_{{\text{PB}}}=m\;,\qquad[{p}\,,\,\bar{\tilde{\tilde{\mathfrak{d}}}}]_{{\text{PB}}}=m\;,\qquad\{d_{\bar{w}},\bar{d}_{w}\}_{{\text{PB}}}=4im\;,

(I.29)

reflecting their second class nature, and the weakly vanishing brackets

\displaystyle{}[\tilde{\tilde{\mathfrak{d}}}\,,\,\bar{\tilde{\tilde{\mathfrak{d}}}}]_{{\text{PB}}}=\tilde{\tilde{U}}{}^{(0)}+2\text{x}_{A}\bar{p}-2\bar{\text{x}}_{A}p+\bar{\theta}^{w}\bar{d}_{w}-{\theta}^{\bar{w}}d_{\bar{w}}\;\approx 0,\qquad

(I.30)

\displaystyle{}[\tilde{\tilde{\mathfrak{d}}}\,,\,d_{\bar{w}}]_{{\text{PB}}}=2i\bar{\theta}^{w}p\approx 0\;,\qquad{}[\tilde{\tilde{\mathfrak{d}}}\,,\,d_{\bar{w}}]_{{\text{PB}}}=-2i{\theta}^{\bar{w}}{p}\approx 0\;,\qquad

(I.31)

\displaystyle{}[\bar{\tilde{\tilde{\mathfrak{d}}}}\,,\,d_{\bar{w}}]_{{\text{PB}}}=-2i\bar{\theta}^{w}\bar{p}\approx 0\;,\qquad{}[\bar{\tilde{\tilde{\mathfrak{d}}}}\,,\,d_{\bar{w}}]_{{\text{PB}}}=2i{\theta}^{\bar{w}}\bar{p}\approx 0\;.\qquad

(I.32)

In summary, the algebra of the constraints is presented in Table

The bosonic second class constraints in (
I.26
) are now explicitly solved with respect to
{\rm x}_{A}
,
\bar{{\rm x}}_{A}
and their conjugate momenta. Thus a consistent way is to use these to reduce the phase space of our dynamical system before quantization. An equivalent approach is provided by the (classical counterpart of) generalized/deformed Gupta-Bleuler procedure which implies that we impose only half of the second- lass constraints on the quantum system
p\approx 0
and
\bar{p}\approx 0
. At the classical level, this implies omitting the conjugate constraints
\bar{\tilde{\tilde{\mathfrak{d}}}}\approx 0
and
\tilde{\tilde{{\mathfrak{d}}}}\approx 0
from consideration, thus converting their conjugate constraints
p\approx 0
and
\bar{p}\approx 0
into first class constraints
65
65

65

Notice that this is a deformed version of Gupta-Bleuler quantization. As we have already written in the main text and shown in appendix
H
, the canonical Gupta-Bleuler approach to the bosonic second class constraints of our system fails to produce the correct result equivalent to the Dirac bracket quantization.
.

We prefer this latter treatment of the bosonic second class constraints because it aligns with the (canonical) Gupta-Bleuler treatment of the fermionic second class constraints in (
I.26
). These are complex conjugate pairs and, after quantization, we will impose on the state vector the quantum counterpart of only one of two fermionic second class constraints,
\bar{d}_{w}\approx 0
. This is equivalent to omitting, at the classical level, the conjugate
d_{\bar{w}}\approx 0
constraint, which converts
\bar{d}_{w}\approx 0
into a first class constraint. The resulting algebra of the effective first class constraints appearing as a result of the implementation of the above classical counterpart of the generalized/deformed Gupta-Bleuler procedure is presented in Table
I.2
.
