To quantize our dynamical system in the supercoordinate representation we represent our effective first class constraints by differential operators,

\hat{\Phi}^{(0)}=-i\partial_{\text{x}^{(0)}}-m\;,

(I.33)

\hat{\bar{\Phi}}=-i\partial_{\text{x}_{A}}\;,\qquad\hat{{\Phi}}=-i\bar{\partial}_{\bar{\text{x}}_{A}}\;,

(I.34)

\begin{array}[]{l}\begin{split}\hat{\tilde{\tilde{U}}}{}^{(0)}&=-i\left({\mathbb{D}}^{(0)}+4m\theta^{\bar{w}}\bar{\theta}^{w}-2\text{x}_{A}\partial_{\text{x}_{A}}+2\bar{\text{x}}_{A}\partial_{\bar{\text{x}}_{A}}-\bar{\theta}^{w}\bar{d}_{w}+{\theta}^{\bar{w}}d_{\bar{w}}-q\right)=\\
&=-i\left({\mathbb{D}}^{(0)}-2\text{x}_{A}\partial_{\text{x}_{A}}+2\bar{\text{x}}_{A}\partial_{\bar{\text{x}}_{A}}-\bar{\theta}^{w}\partial_{\bar{\theta}^{{w}}}+{\theta}^{\bar{w}}\partial_{{\theta}^{\bar{w}}}-q\right)\;,\end{split}\end{array}

(I.35)

\hat{d}_{w}=-i{\partial}_{{\theta}{}^{w}}\;,

(I.36)

\hat{\bar{d}}_{\bar{w}}=-i\bar{\partial}_{\bar{\theta}{}^{\bar{w}}}\;,

(I.37)

\hat{\bar{d}}_{w}=-i\left(\bar{\partial}_{\bar{\theta}{}^{w}}-2m{\theta}^{\bar{w}}\right)

(I.38)

and impose them on the state vector. In (
I.33
)-(
I.38
) the derivatives and covariant derivatives are defined as follows

\displaystyle\partial_{\text{x}^{(0)}}=\frac{\partial}{\partial\text{x}^{(0)}}\;,\qquad\partial_{{\text{x}_{A}}}=\frac{\partial}{\partial{\text{x}_{A}}}\;,\qquad\partial_{\bar{\text{x}}_{A}}=\frac{\partial}{\partial\bar{\text{x}}_{A}}\;,\qquad

(I.39)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{D}}^{(0)}=\bar{w}_{\alpha}\frac{\partial}{\partial\bar{w}_{\alpha}}-{w}_{\alpha}\frac{\partial}{\partial{w}_{\alpha}}\;,\qquad{\mathbb{D}}=w_{\alpha}\frac{\partial}{\partial\bar{w}_{\alpha}}\;,\qquad\bar{{\mathbb{D}}}=\bar{w}_{\alpha}\frac{\partial}{\partial{w}_{\alpha}}\;.

(I.40)

Notice the appearance of the ordering constant in the homogeneous differential operator (
I.35
).

To understand the structure of the fermionic constraint (
I.38
) it is useful to keep in mind
the relation between derivatives and covariant derivatives in the central and analytical basis. This relationship can be derived from the identity

\begin{array}[]{l}\begin{split}\text{d}&=\text{d}x^{a}\partial_{a}+\text{d}\theta^{\alpha}\partial_{\alpha}+\text{d}\bar{\theta}{}^{\alpha}\bar{\partial}_{\alpha}+ia{\mathbb{D}}^{(0)}+if\bar{{\mathbb{D}}}-i\bar{f}{\mathbb{D}}=\\
&=\Pi^{a}\partial_{a}+\text{d}\theta^{\alpha}\text{D}_{\alpha}+\text{d}\bar{\theta}{}^{\alpha}\bar{\text{D}}_{\alpha}+ia{\mathbb{D}}^{(0)}+if\bar{{\mathbb{D}}}-i\bar{f}{\mathbb{D}}\penalty\ =\\
&=\text{d}{\rm x}^{(0)}\partial_{{\rm x}^{0}}+\text{d}{\rm x}_{A}\partial_{{\rm x}_{A}}+\text{d}\bar{{\rm x}}_{A}\partial_{\bar{{\rm x}}_{A}}+\text{d}\theta^{w}\partial_{\theta^{w}}+\text{d}\theta^{\bar{w}}\partial_{\theta^{\bar{w}}}+\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ +\text{d}\bar{\theta}{}^{w}\partial_{\bar{\theta}{}^{w}}+\text{d}\bar{\theta}{}^{\bar{w}}\partial_{\bar{\theta}{}^{\bar{w}}}+ia\tilde{\mathbb{D}}^{(0)}+if\bar{\tilde{\mathbb{D}}}-i\bar{f}\tilde{\mathbb{D}}\,.\end{split}\end{array}

(I.41)

This identity implies, in particular,

\displaystyle\partial_{a}=u_{a}^{(0)}\partial_{{\rm x}^{(0)}}+u_{a}\partial_{{\rm x}_{A}}+\bar{u}_{a}\partial_{\bar{{\rm x}}_{A}}\;,\qquad

(I.42)

\displaystyle\text{D}_{\alpha}=\partial_{\alpha}+i(\gamma^{a}\bar{\theta})_{\alpha}=w_{\alpha}\left(\partial_{\theta^{w}}+i\bar{\theta}{}^{\bar{w}}\partial_{{\rm x}^{(0)}}\right)+\bar{w}_{\alpha}\left(\partial_{\theta^{\bar{w}}}+4i\bar{\theta}{}^{\bar{w}}\partial_{\bar{\rm x}_{A}}+i\bar{\theta}{}^{{w}}\partial_{{\rm x}^{(0)}}\right)\;,

(I.43)

\displaystyle\bar{\text{D}}_{\alpha}=\bar{\partial}_{\alpha}+i(\gamma^{a}{\theta})_{\alpha}=w_{\alpha}\left(\bar{\partial}_{\bar{\theta}{}^{w}}+4i{\theta}{}^{w}\partial_{{\rm x}_{A}}+i{\theta}{}^{\bar{w}}\partial_{{\rm x}^{(0)}}\right)+\bar{w}_{\alpha}\left(\bar{\partial}_{\bar{\theta}^{\bar{w}}}+i{\theta}{}^{w}\partial_{{\rm x}^{(0)}}\right)\;.

(I.44)

The last equation can be used to write (
I.38
) in the form

\hat{\bar{d}}_{w}=-\bar{w}\bar{\text{D}}+im{\theta}^{\bar{w}}-4i\theta^{w}\hat{\Phi}-i\theta^{\bar{w}}\hat{\Phi}{}^{(0)}\;.

(I.45)

Appendix J
Born-Oppenheimer-like approach to asymptotic form of a solution for
N
=2 3D mD0 system

Following
[
187
]
, we can search for an asymptotic form of the solution of Eqs. (
7.260
)-(
7.269
) using the so-called
tabular
or
endpoint coordinates
(see
[
187
]
for references)

Z^{I}=X^{I}e^{i\beta}+|X|^{-1/2}z^{I}\;,\qquad\bar{Z}{}^{I}=X^{I}e^{-i\beta}+|X|^{-1/2}\bar{z}{}^{I}\;,\qquad

(J.1)

in the neighbourhood of the classical vacuum configuration

Z^{I}=X^{I}e^{i\beta}\penalty\ ,\qquad\bar{Z}{}^{I}=X^{I}e^{-i\beta}\penalty\ ,

(J.2)

which represents a general solution to the condition for vanishing YM potential which reduces to
\epsilon_{IJK}Z^{J}\bar{Z}{}^{K}=0
. In (
J.1
)
X^{I}
is real,
X^{I}=(X^{I})^{*}
, with
|X|=\sqrt{X^{I}X^{I}}
, and the complex variables
z^{I}=(\bar{z}{}^{I})^{*}
are orthogonal to
X^{I}
. Additionally, they satisfy two constraints, which we choose as
\Re{\rm e}(z^{I}e^{-i\beta})=0
,

X^{I}z^{I}=0=X^{I}\bar{z}{}^{I}\;\;,\qquad z^{I}e^{-i\beta}+\bar{z}^{I}e^{i\beta}=0\;.

(J.3)

The asymptotic regime corresponds to large
|X|
, formally

r=|X|=\sqrt{X^{I}X^{I}}\mapsto\infty\;,

(J.4)

with
z^{I}\bar{z}{}^{I}
kept to be finite. In our case, it is convenient to solve (
J.3
) by setting
z^{I}=iy^{I}e^{i\beta}
in terms of a real vector
y^{I}
, yielding
\bar{z}^{I}=-iy^{I}e^{-i\beta}
. Thus, the above coordinate system is finally described by

\displaystyle Z^{I}=(rn^{I}+ir^{-1/2}y^{I})e^{i\beta}\;,\qquad\bar{Z}{}^{I}=(rn^{I}-ir^{-1/2}y{}^{I})e^{-i\beta}\;,\qquad

(J.5)

\displaystyle r:=|X|=\sqrt{X^{I}X^{I}}\;,\qquad n^{I}=X^{I}/r=X^{I}/|X|\quad\Rightarrow\quad n^{I}n^{I}=1\;,\qquad

(J.6)

\displaystyle y^{I}=(y^{I})^{*}\;,\qquad n^{I}y^{I}=0\;.\qquad

(J.7)

To proceed, it is convenient to introduce complex null-vectors that complete the unit vector
n^{I}
till a nondegenerate SO
(3)
frame, i.e. vectors
U^{I}
and
\bar{U}^{I}=(U^{I})^{*}
which obeys

n^{I}U^{I}=0\;,\qquad n^{I}\bar{U}^{I}=0\;,\qquad U^{I}U^{I}=0\;,\qquad\bar{U}^{I}\bar{U}^{I}=0\;,\qquad U^{I}\bar{U}^{I}=1\;.

(J.8)

Then,

\delta^{IJ}=n^{I}n^{J}+U^{I}\bar{U}^{J}+\bar{U}^{I}U^{J}\;\qquad

(J.9)

and the constraint (
J.7
) can be solved by

y^{I}=y_{U}\bar{U}^{I}+y_{\bar{U}}U^{I}\;,\qquad{\text{so that}}\qquad y^{I}y^{I}=|y_{U}|^{2}\;.

(J.10)

We fix the orientation of the SO
(3)
frame by setting
\epsilon_{IJK}\bar{U}^{I}U^{J}n^{K}=i
, which implies

\epsilon_{IJK}\bar{U}^{J}n^{K}=-i\bar{U}^{I}\;,\qquad\epsilon_{IJK}{U}^{J}n^{K}=i{U}^{I}\;.\qquad

(J.11)

Using these relations, we find

\epsilon_{IJK}Z^{J}\bar{Z}^{K}=2\sqrt{r}(y_{U}\bar{U}{}^{I}-y_{\bar{U}}U^{I})=2i\sqrt{r}\epsilon_{IJK}y^{J}n^{K}\;.

(J.12)

The derivatives of the constrained vectors can be expressed in terms of three Cartan forms, defines as

\Omega_{1}=U^{I}\text{d}n^{I}\,,\qquad\bar{\Omega}_{1}=\bar{U}^{I}\text{d}n^{I}\qquad\text{ and}\qquad\Omega_{1}^{(0)}=\bar{U}^{I}\text{d}{U}^{I}\;.

(J.13)

These allow us to write

\text{d}n^{I}=U^{I}\bar{\Omega}_{1}+\bar{U}^{I}\Omega_{1}\;,\qquad\text{d}{U}^{I}={U}^{I}\Omega_{1}^{(0)}-n^{I}\Omega_{1}\;,\qquad\text{d}\bar{U}^{I}=-\bar{U}^{I}\Omega_{1}^{(0)}-n^{I}\bar{\Omega}_{1}\;.\qquad

(J.14)

Using these, the derivative of the coordinates
y^{I}
can be expressed as

\text{d}y^{I}=(\text{d}y_{U}-y_{U}\Omega_{1}^{(0)})\bar{U}^{I}+(\text{d}y_{\bar{U}}+y_{\bar{U}}\Omega_{1}^{(0)})U^{I}-n^{I}(y_{U}\bar{\Omega}_{1}+y_{\bar{U}}\Omega_{1})\;.

(J.15)

Now, using equations (
J.5
), (
J.10
), (
J.14
), (
J.15
) we can compute d
Z^{I}
and d
\bar{Z}{}^{I}
, and subsequently decompose the differential operator

\text{d}=\text{d}Z^{I}\partial_{I}+\text{d}\bar{Z}{}^{I}\bar{\partial}_{I}=\text{d}r\partial_{r}+\text{d}\beta\partial_{(\beta)}+\text{d}y^{I}\partial^{y}_{I}+\text{d}n^{I}\partial_{n^{I}}

(J.16)

on the basis of six independent 1-forms:
\text{d}r
,
\text{d}\beta
,
\text{d}y_{U}
,
\text{d}y_{\bar{U}}
,
\Omega_{1}
,
\bar{\Omega}_{1}

66
66

66

\Omega^{(0)}
also appears in intermediate expressions; however, its coefficient does not produce independent equations, reflecting the underlying U
(1)
gauge symmetry of the construction.
. In this way, we obtain the following expressions for derivatives in the tabular (endpoint) coordinates

\displaystyle\partial_{r}=n^{I}(e^{i\beta}\partial_{I}+e^{-i\beta}\bar{\partial}_{I})-\frac{i}{2}\,\frac{1}{r^{3/2}}\,(y_{U}\bar{U}^{I}+y_{\bar{U}}U^{I})\,(e^{i\beta}\partial_{I}-e^{-i\beta}\bar{\partial}_{I})\;,\qquad

(J.17)

\displaystyle\partial_{(\beta)}=irn^{I}(e^{i\beta}\partial_{I}-e^{-i\beta}\bar{\partial}_{I})-\frac{1}{r^{1/2}}\,(y_{U}\bar{U}^{I}+y_{\bar{U}}U^{I})\,(e^{i\beta}\partial_{I}+e^{-i\beta}\bar{\partial}_{I})\;,\qquad

(J.18)

\displaystyle\bar{U}^{I}\left(\partial^{y}_{I}-\frac{i}{r^{1/2}}(e^{i\beta}\partial_{I}-e^{-i\beta}\bar{\partial}_{I})\right)=0\,,\qquad

(J.19)

\displaystyle{U}^{I}\left(\partial^{y}_{I}-\frac{i}{r^{1/2}}(e^{i\beta}\partial_{I}-e^{-i\beta}\bar{\partial}_{I})\right)=0\,,\qquad

(J.20)

\displaystyle\bar{U}^{I}\left(\partial_{n^{I}}-r(e^{i\beta}\partial_{I}+e^{-i\beta}\bar{\partial}_{I})\right)-y_{\bar{U}}n^{I}\left(\partial^{y}_{I}-\frac{i}{r^{1/2}}(e^{i\beta}\partial_{I}-e^{-i\beta}\bar{\partial}_{I})\right)=0\,,\qquad

(J.21)

\displaystyle{U}^{I}\left(\partial_{n^{I}}-r(e^{i\beta}\partial_{I}+e^{-i\beta}\bar{\partial}_{I})\right)-y_{U}n^{I}\left(\partial^{y}_{I}-\frac{i}{r^{1/2}}(e^{i\beta}\partial_{I}-e^{-i\beta}\bar{\partial}_{I})\right)=0\,.\qquad

(J.22)

These relations are exact. However, to solve them for
{\partial}_{I}
and
\bar{\partial}_{I}
, we need to use the condition
r\mapsto\infty
of asymptotic regime. To this end let us first rewrite Eqs. (
J.17
)-(
J.22
) in the form convenient for perturbative solution of the system for

\partial^{\pm}_{I}=e^{i\beta}\partial_{I}\pm e^{-i\beta}\bar{\partial}_{I}\;.\qquad

(J.23)

Firstly, from (
J.18
), (
J.19
) and (
J.20
), we find

\displaystyle n^{I}\partial^{-}_{I}=-\frac{i}{r}\partial_{(\beta)}-\frac{i}{r\sqrt{r}}(y_{U}\bar{U}^{I}+y_{\bar{U}}U^{I})\,\partial^{+}_{I}\;,\qquad

(J.24)

\displaystyle\bar{U}^{I}\partial^{-}_{I}=-i\sqrt{r}\bar{U}^{I}\partial^{y}_{I}\;,\qquad{U}^{I}\partial^{-}_{I}=-i\sqrt{r}{U}^{I}\partial^{y}_{I}\;.\qquad

(J.25)

Using (
J.9
), these can be unified in one equation

\partial^{-}_{I}=-i\sqrt{r}(\delta^{IJ}-n^{I}n^{J})\partial^{y}_{J}-\frac{i}{r}n^{I}\partial_{(\beta)}-\frac{i}{r\sqrt{r}}n^{I}(y_{U}\bar{U}^{J}+y_{\bar{U}}U^{J})\,\partial^{+}_{J}\;.\qquad

(J.26)

Now, using these expressions, we can solve formally Eqs. (
J.17
), (
J.21
) and (
J.22
) with respect to projections of
\partial^{+}_{I}
:

\displaystyle n^{I}\partial^{+}_{I}=\partial_{r}+\frac{1}{2r}\,(y_{U}\bar{U}^{J}+y_{\bar{U}}U^{J})\,\partial^{y}_{J}\;,\qquad

(J.27)

\displaystyle\bar{U}^{I}\partial^{+}_{I}=\frac{1}{r}\,\bar{U}^{I}\partial_{n^{I}}-\frac{1}{r}\,y_{\bar{U}}n^{I}\partial^{y}_{I}+\frac{1}{r^{2}\sqrt{r}}y_{\bar{U}}\,\partial_{(\beta)}+\frac{1}{r^{3}}y_{\bar{U}}\,(y_{U}\bar{U}^{J}+y_{\bar{U}}U^{J})\,\partial^{+}_{J}\,,\qquad

(J.28)

\displaystyle{U}^{I}\partial^{+}_{I}=\frac{1}{r}\,{U}^{I}\partial_{n^{I}}-\frac{1}{r}\,y_{U}n^{I}\partial^{y}_{I}++\frac{1}{r^{2}\sqrt{r}}y_{{U}}\,\partial_{(\beta)}+\frac{1}{r^{3}}y_{{U}}\,(y_{U}\bar{U}^{J}+y_{\bar{U}}U^{J})\,\partial^{+}_{J}\,.\qquad

(J.29)

Note that, according to (
J.28
) and (
J.29
),
(y_{U}\bar{U}^{J}+y_{\bar{U}}U^{J})\,\partial^{+}_{J}\propto\frac{1}{r}
and the derivative
\partial_{r}
only appears in the first term of (
J.27
). Using these observations and repeatedly applying (
J.9
), we obtain the following approximate solution

\displaystyle\partial^{+}_{I}=\frac{1}{r}\left[n^{I}\left(r\,\partial_{r}+\frac{1}{2}y^{J}\partial^{y}_{J}\right)+(\delta^{IJ}-n^{I}n^{J})\,\partial_{n^{J}}+y^{I}n^{J}\partial^{y}_{J}\right]+{\cal O}(r^{-5/2})\;,\qquad

(J.30)

\displaystyle\partial^{-}_{I}=-i\sqrt{r}(\delta^{IJ}-n^{I}n^{J})\partial^{y}_{J}-\frac{i}{r}n^{I}\partial_{(\beta)}+{\cal O}(r^{-5/2})\;\qquad

(J.31)

in which the
{\cal O}(r^{-5/2})
terms do not involve
\partial_{r}
derivative.

Finally, from (
J.23
) we easily find

\begin{array}[]{l}\begin{split}\partial_{I}&=\frac{1}{2}e^{-i\beta}\Bigg[-i\sqrt{r}(\delta^{IJ}-n^{I}n^{J})\partial^{y}_{J}+\frac{1}{r}\left(n^{I}\left(r\,\partial_{r}-i\partial_{(\beta)}+\frac{1}{2}y^{J}\partial^{y}_{J}\right)+\right.\\
&+(\delta^{IJ}-n^{I}n^{J})\,\partial_{n^{J}}+y^{I}n^{J}\partial^{y}_{J}\biggr)\Bigg]+{\cal O}(r^{-5/2})\;,\end{split}\end{array}

(J.32)

\begin{array}[]{l}\begin{split}\bar{\partial}_{I}&=\frac{1}{2}e^{i\beta}\Bigg[+i\sqrt{r}(\delta^{IJ}-n^{I}n^{J})\partial^{y}_{J}+\frac{1}{r}\left(n^{I}\left(r\,\partial_{r}+i\partial_{(\beta)}+\frac{1}{2}y^{J}\partial^{y}_{J}\right)+\right.\\
&+(\delta^{IJ}-n^{I}n^{J})\,\partial_{n^{J}}+y^{I}n^{J}\partial^{y}_{J}\biggr)\Bigg]+{\cal O}(r^{-5/2})\;.\end{split}\end{array}

(J.33)

Notice that in the asymptotic region (
J.4
),
r\mapsto\infty
, the leading order in the above decomposition of the derivatives is
\propto\sqrt{r}
,

\displaystyle\partial_{I}=-\frac{i}{2}e^{-i\beta}\sqrt{r}(\delta^{IJ}-n^{I}n^{J})\partial^{y}_{J}+{\cal O}(r^{-1})\;,\qquad

(J.34)

\displaystyle\bar{\partial}_{I}=\frac{i}{2}e^{i\beta}\sqrt{r}(\delta^{IJ}-n^{I}n^{J})\partial^{y}_{J}+{\cal O}(r^{-1})\;,\qquad

(J.35)

so that

\displaystyle n^{I}\partial_{I}=0+{\cal O}(r^{-1})\;,\qquad

(J.36)

\displaystyle n^{I}\bar{\partial}_{I}=0+{\cal O}(r^{-1})\;.\qquad

(J.37)

According to (
J.12
), the factor
\sqrt{r}
also appears in all nonvanishing r.h.s.’s of Eqs (
7.260
)-(
7.265
) and (
7.267
)-(
7.269
), which can be written in the form

\displaystyle 2i\sqrt{r}\epsilon_{IJK}y^{J}n^{K}{\mathfrak{f}}_{I}^{(q-1)}=0\qquad\Longleftrightarrow\qquad y_{U}\bar{U}^{I}{\mathfrak{f}}_{I}^{(q-1)}=y_{\bar{U}}U^{I}{\mathfrak{f}}_{I}^{(q-1)}\;,\qquad

(J.38)

\displaystyle\bar{\partial}_{I}{\mathfrak{f}}^{(q)}=8i\sqrt{2}\sqrt{r}\epsilon_{JKL}y^{J}n^{K}\bar{\partial}_{[L}{\mathfrak{f}}_{I]}{}^{(q)}\;,\qquad

(J.39)

\displaystyle\bar{\partial}_{[I}{\mathfrak{f}}_{J]}{}^{(q-1)}=8i\sqrt{2}\sqrt{r}y^{[I}n^{J]}{\mathfrak{f}}^{(q-3)}\;\qquad

(J.40)

and

\displaystyle\partial_{I}{\mathfrak{f}}^{(q-1)}_{I}=0\;,\qquad

(J.41)

\displaystyle\partial_{J}\bar{\partial}_{[I}{\mathfrak{f}}^{(q)}_{J]}=-\frac{i}{\sqrt{2}\mu^{12}}\,\sqrt{r}\,\epsilon_{IJK}y^{J}n^{K}f^{(q)}\;,

(J.42)

\displaystyle\partial_{I}{\mathfrak{f}}^{(q-3)}=-\frac{i\sqrt{2}}{\mu^{12}}\,\sqrt{r}\,y^{[I}n{}^{J]}{\mathfrak{f}}^{(q-1)}_{J}\;.\qquad

(J.43)

The algebraic equation (
J.38
) can be easily solved by

{\mathfrak{f}}^{(q-1)}_{I}=n^{I}{\mathfrak{f}}^{(q-1)}_{(n)}+y^{I}{\mathfrak{f}}^{(q-1)}_{(y)}\;.\qquad

(J.44)

We can now decompose the “wavefunctions” into inverse powers of
r\sqrt{r}
and try to solve the equations order by order. To be consistent, we set

{\mathfrak{f}}^{(q\ldots)}_{\ldots}=r^{-k}\left({\mathfrak{f}}^{(q\ldots)}_{\ldots[0]}+\frac{1}{r\sqrt{r}}{\mathfrak{f}}^{(q\ldots)}_{\ldots[1]}+\frac{1}{r^{3}}{\mathfrak{f}}^{(q\ldots)}_{\ldots[2]}+\ldots\right)\;,

(J.45)

with some
k
to be determined, for all but the leading components of the state vector superfield. For the leading component, we instead assume

{\mathfrak{f}}^{(q)}=\sqrt{r}{\mathfrak{f}}^{(q)}_{[0]}+\frac{1}{r}{\mathfrak{f}}^{(q)}_{[1]}+\frac{1}{r^{2}}{\mathfrak{f}}^{(q)}_{[2]}+\ldots\;

(J.46)

Here we focus on the zeroth order, where (following the terms
\propto\sqrt{r}
) we obtain the equations

\displaystyle(\delta^{IJ}-n^{I}n^{J})\partial^{y}_{J}{\mathfrak{f}}^{(q)}_{[0]}=8i\sqrt{2}\epsilon_{JKL}y^{J}n^{K}(\delta^{[L|P}-n^{[L}n^{P})\partial^{y}_{P}{\mathfrak{f}}_{I][0]}{}^{(q)}\;,\qquad

(J.47)

\displaystyle(\delta^{[I|K}-n^{[I|}n^{K})\partial^{y}_{K}{\mathfrak{f}}_{J][0]}{}^{(q-1)}=16\sqrt{2}e^{-i\beta}y^{[I}n^{J]}{\mathfrak{f}}^{(q-3)}_{[0]}\;,\qquad

(J.48)

\displaystyle(\delta^{IJ}-n^{I}n^{J})\partial^{y}_{J}{\mathfrak{f}}^{(q-1)}_{I[0]}=0\;,\qquad

(J.49)

\displaystyle(\delta^{JK}-n^{J}n^{K})\partial^{y}_{K}\,(\delta^{[I|L}-n^{[I|}n^{L})\partial^{y}_{L}{\mathfrak{f}}^{(q)}_{|J][0]}=-\frac{2\sqrt{2}\,i}{(\mu^{6})^{2}}\,\,\epsilon_{IJK}y^{J}n^{K}f^{(q)}_{[0]}\;,

(J.50)

\displaystyle(\delta^{IJ}-n^{I}n^{J})\partial^{y}_{J}{\mathfrak{f}}^{(q-3)}_{[0]}=\frac{2\sqrt{2}}{(\mu^{6})^{2}}\,e^{i\beta}\,y^{[I}n{}^{J]}{\mathfrak{f}}^{(q-1)}_{J[0]}\;.\qquad

(J.51)

Observe that this system of equations involves only derivatives with respect to the variables
y^{I}
. With this in mind, one immediately deduces that (
J.47
) implies

\epsilon_{JKL}y^{J}n^{K}(\delta^{LP}-n^{L}n^{P})\partial^{y}_{P}(n^{I}{\mathfrak{f}}_{I[0]}{}^{(q)})=0\;,\qquad

(J.52)

which indicates that
n^{I}{\mathfrak{f}}_{I[0]}{}^{(q)}
depends on
y^{J}
only through its square,
|y|^{2}=y^{J}y^{J}
.

Eq. (
J.49
), together with leading order term of (
J.44
),

{\mathfrak{f}}^{(q-1)}_{I[0]}=n^{I}{\mathfrak{f}}^{(q-1)}_{(n)[0]}+y^{I}{\mathfrak{f}}^{(q-1)}_{(y)[0]}

(J.53)

does not impose any condition of
{\mathfrak{f}}^{(q-1)}_{(n)[0]}
, but requires
{\mathfrak{f}}^{(q-1)}_{(y)[0]}
to satisfy
(y^{I}\partial^{y}_{I}+2){\mathfrak{f}}^{(q-1)}_{(y)[0]}=0
. Nevertheless, as it will become clear shortly, we have to choose the trivial solution of this equation. Indeed, substituting (
J.53
) into (
J.51
), we find

\displaystyle(\delta^{IJ}-n^{I}n^{J})\partial^{y}_{J}{\mathfrak{f}}^{(q-3)}_{[0]}=\frac{\sqrt{2}}{(\mu^{6})^{2}}\,e^{i\beta}\,\left(y^{I}{\mathfrak{f}}^{(q-1)}_{(n)[0]}-n{}^{I}(y^{J}y^{J}){\mathfrak{f}}^{(q-1)}_{y[0]}\right)\;.\qquad

(J.54)

Since both l.h.s. and the first term on the r.h.s. of this equation vanish when contracted with
n^{I}
, the last term must vanish independently,

{\mathfrak{f}}^{(q-1)}_{y[0]}=0\qquad\Longrightarrow\qquad{\mathfrak{f}}^{(q-1)}_{I[0]}=n^{I}{\mathfrak{f}}^{(q-1)}_{(n)[0]}\;.

(J.55)

Then, Eq. (
J.54
) reduces to

\displaystyle(\delta^{IJ}-n^{I}n^{J})\partial^{y}_{J}{\mathfrak{f}}^{(q-3)}_{[0]}=\frac{\sqrt{2}}{(\mu^{6})^{2}}\,e^{i\beta}\,y^{I}{\mathfrak{f}}^{(q-1)}_{(n)[0]}\;.\qquad

(J.56)

This equation implies that both
{\mathfrak{f}}^{(q-3)}_{[0]}
and
{\mathfrak{f}}^{(q-1)}_{(n)[0]}
depend on
y^{I}
vector only through its length
|y|=\sqrt{y^{I}y^{I}}
. Therefore,

\partial_{|y|^{2}}{\mathfrak{f}}^{(q-3)}_{[0]}=\frac{1}{(\mu^{6})^{2}\sqrt{2}}\,e^{i\beta}\,{\mathfrak{f}}^{(q-1)}_{(n)[0]}\;.\qquad

(J.57)

On the other hand, with the above conclusion on dependence on
y^{I}
vector only through its length
|y|=\sqrt{y^{I}y^{I}}
, Eq. (
J.40
) reduces to

\partial_{|y|^{2}}{\mathfrak{f}}^{(q-1)}_{(n)[0]}=8\sqrt{2}\,e^{-i\beta}\,{\mathfrak{f}}^{(q-3)}_{[0]}\penalty\ .

(J.58)

Using this and (
J.57
), we find a simple equation

\frac{\partial}{\partial|y|^{2}}\frac{\partial}{\partial|y|^{2}}{\mathfrak{f}}^{(q-1)}_{(n)[0]}=\frac{8}{(\mu^{6})^{2}}\,{\mathfrak{f}}^{(q-1)}_{(n)[0]}\;\qquad

(J.59)

which is solved by

{\mathfrak{f}}^{(q-1)}_{(n)[0]}={\mathfrak{h}}^{(q-1)}(r,\beta,\vec{n})\;{\rm exp}\left(-2\sqrt{2}\frac{|y|^{2}}{\mu^{6}}\right)\;

(J.60)

with some function
{\mathfrak{h}}^{(q-1)}(r,\beta,\vec{n})
depending on the remaining coordinates.

Similarly one can study the case of other components of the state vector superfield, whose statistics (bosonic if the superfield is bosonic) are opposite to the components discussed above (fermionic if the superfield is bosonic), which obey Eqs. (
J.47
) and (
J.50
). Since the study of this case is somewhat more involved than previous one, we simplify the analysis by choosing a special frame in which
n^{I}=(0,0,1)=\delta^{I}_{3}
, and consequently
y^{I}=(y^{1},y^{2},0)=\delta^{I}_{r}y^{r}
.

In this SO
(3)
frame, the solution to (
J.52
) can be written in the form
{\mathfrak{f}}_{3[0]}{}^{(q)}(y^{J})={\mathfrak{f}}_{3[0]}{}^{(q)}(|y|^{2})
, and the remaining components of (
J.47
) and (
J.50
) simplify to

\displaystyle\partial^{y}_{r}{\mathfrak{f}}^{(q)}_{[0]}=8i\sqrt{2}\epsilon_{st}y^{t}\partial^{y}_{[s}{\mathfrak{f}}_{r][0]}{}^{(q)}\;,\qquad

(J.61)

\displaystyle\partial^{y}_{s}\,\partial^{y}_{[r}{\mathfrak{f}}^{(q)}_{s][0]}=-\frac{i2\sqrt{2}}{(\mu^{6})^{2}}\,\,\epsilon_{rs}y^{s}{\mathfrak{f}}^{(q)}_{[0]}\;\;.\qquad

(J.62)

Furthermore, since
r,s,t=1,2
, the antisymmetric derivative can be expressed as
\partial^{y}_{[s}{\mathfrak{f}}_{r][0]}{}^{(q)}=\epsilon_{sr}\tilde{{\mathfrak{f}}}^{(q)}_{[0]}
, where

\tilde{{\mathfrak{f}}}^{(q)}_{[0]}=\frac{1}{2}\epsilon_{sr}\partial^{y}_{[s}{\mathfrak{f}}_{r][0]}{}^{(q)}
and the above equations reduce to

\partial^{y}_{r}{\mathfrak{f}}^{(q)}_{[0]}=8i\sqrt{2}y^{r}\tilde{{\mathfrak{f}}}^{(q)}_{[0]}\;,\qquad\partial^{y}_{s}\,\tilde{{\mathfrak{f}}}^{(q)}_{[0]}=-\frac{2\sqrt{2}i}{(\mu^{6})^{2}}\,y^{s}\,{\mathfrak{f}}^{(q)}_{[0]}\;.\qquad

(J.63)

It follows immediately that both
{\mathfrak{f}}^{(q)}_{[0]}
and
\tilde{{\mathfrak{f}}}^{(q)}_{[0]}
depend on
y^{I}
only through its square
|y|^{2}=y^{I}y^{I}
, and that they obey the second order linear differential equation in
|y|^{2}
,

\partial_{|y|^{2}}\partial_{|y|^{2}}{\mathfrak{f}}^{(q)}_{[0]}=\frac{8}{(\mu^{6})^{2}}{\mathfrak{f}}^{(q)}_{[0]}\;,

(J.64)

which is solved by

{\mathfrak{f}}^{(q)}_{[0]}={\mathfrak{h}}^{(q)}_{[0]}(r,\beta,\vec{n})\exp\left(-\frac{2\sqrt{2}}{\mu^{6}}\,|y|^{2}\right)\;,

(J.65)

where
{\mathfrak{h}}^{(q)}_{[0]}(r,\beta,\vec{n})
is a function of the remaining variables.

We have chosen the negative sign in the exponent in both (
J.60
) and (
J.65
) to ensure that the wavefunction is convergent in
y^{I}
.

We will not consider higher orders of the Born-Oppenheimer-like approximation, since this goes beyond the scope of the present thesis; we refer the reader to
[
187
]
for a detailed treatment of such corrections. It is worth noting that in that work, besides the case of Matrix model obtained by dimensional reduction of
\text{D}=3
SYM model to
\text{d}=1
, Matrix models from reductions of higher dimensional SYM were also studied, with particular attention drawn to the exceptional properties of
\text{D}=10
case. This will appear in a setup similar to (
7.251
)-(
7.253
) from the field theory of our 10D mD
0
model which we aim to quantize in future work.

1-Hitzaurrea/atzea.pdf
